  promotion trigger met: org itself overloads level
:END:

Parked 2026-08-19, *not accepted* — recorded so the research need not be
redone, not because a case was made.

*Verified in this Emacs:* =:CATEGORY:= on a level-1 heading is reported
by a level-3 grandchild, so one property per top-level heading labels
its whole subtree. A =[/]= cookie on a parent moved =[1/3]= -> =[2/3]=
on a child's state change with no explicit update call, so cookies
self-maintain through the review pass.

*Verified absent from this repo:* =:CATEGORY:=, statistics cookies,
=:VISIBILITY:=, and tag groups — zero uses of each. The level-1
"category" convention is hand-built where org has its own.

*Org practice, from two sources not a consensus:* epic as subtree,
cross-cutting axes as tags — one practitioner tags literally
=:sprint2:= alongside =:release4_2:=. Org's manual treats the tree as
the single structure and everything cross-cutting as a tag or property
match. Sources: [[https://jeffbradberry.com/posts/2023/08/orgmode-epic-organization/][Bradberry, Epic Organization]] and
[[https://orgmode.org/manual/Matching-tags-and-properties.html][Matching tags and properties]].

*Candidate tier naming discussed:* level 1 epic, level 2 story, level 3
task, sprint as a cross-cutting tag. =bin/lint-org='s header would need
updating from "category, task, epic-child".

*Closing action discussed for a heading:* fold historical prose into a
drawer (drawers fold by default; no =:VISIBILITY:= needed) and leave a
short summary — problem, plan, outcome, how verified, what differed from
the plan, what was rejected and why, what it spawned. The last two are
the reused parts and the easiest to drop.

Note [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]] closed the
related flattening question against, on the grounds that archive routing
depends on level-1 =:ARCHIVE:=. That cost is real but is a mapping to be
written, not a wall.

*Composing a story by reference rather than by moving.* Parked
2026-08-20, unreviewed, because it existed only in conversation and
would otherwise be lost. Same standing as the rest of this heading: not
accepted.

*The problem.* A story whose members sit in different categories cannot
be composed as a subtree — level 4 is forbidden, so composing means
*relocating* level-2 headings under a new container.

*Four mechanisms, and the axis that separates them is who declares
membership:*

| mechanism                            | declared at   | drifts? |
|--------------------------------------+---------------+---------|
| =[[id:]]= links in the container body | the container | yes     |
| a =:MEMBERS:= property on it          | the container | yes     |
| a tag on each member                  | *the member*  | no      |
| an id-valued property on each member  | *the member*  | no      |

The top two are the shape that already failed twice here: one edit point
nobody revisits, exactly like the plan file's /"Covers all five
children"/, true when written and false two days later.

*A tag, not a bespoke property* — and that reverses what was recommended
first. The argument for an id-valued =:SPRINT:= property was that a tag
is a string needing rewriting if the story is renamed. That is backwards
for org: stories are named by number or date and are not renamed, and a
tag buys agenda filtering, =org-tags-view=, inheritance and group tags
\(=:startgrouptag:=\) for free, where a property buys none of it without
code. Sources: [[https://jeffbradberry.com/posts/2023/08/orgmode-epic-organization/][Bradberry, Epic Organization]], who tags literally
=:sprint2:= beside =:release4_2:=, and [[https://orgmode.org/manual/Matching-tags-and-properties.html][Matching tags and properties]],
where the manual treats the tree as the single structure and everything
cross-cutting as a match.

*The concrete cost of the alternative, which is the strongest argument
here:* categories carry =:ARCHIVE:=, inherited by their subtree. Moving
a heading across a category boundary to compose a story therefore
changes where it lands when it closes. Composition by relocation
silently rewrites archive routing; a reference changes nothing. Note
this is a *different* claim from
[[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]]'s, which is about
removing categories altogether.

*The case that would test it*, and it is a good one because the odd
member is the important one: the capture cluster is
[[id:bd482c92-a71a-4d7d-88df-47678ee94c79][bd482c92]] in *Tooling* —
the root cause — with
[[id:97696fc2-f07b-4586-b280-de6a7d26fb87][97696fc2]],
[[id:7fa68d5c-b6c0-43df-85c6-764b1d56511f][7fa68d5c]] and
[[id:8ddd7fa8-e847-42e9-b82c-63bbe4c11b7b][8ddd7fa8]] in *Skill logic*.
Composing it by moving would relocate the root cause away from the
category it belongs to, purely to make the story expressible.

*New evidence 2026-08-21, from building the datetree* ([[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]], [[id:cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2][cd1e974e]]). It cuts both ways, which is why this stays parked rather than promoted.

*Against, and worth stating first: depth-as-storage did not break.* =org_outline=, =claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p= and the lint's =org-map-entries= all read level correctly. What broke was reading depth *textually* -- an =awk= parent-inference that returned each heading's own title rather than its parent, which put a wrong claim about this file's structure into conversation before =org_outline= corrected it. A tag is equally mis-readable by a bad grep. That lesson is "read through org", not "change what org stores".

*For, and stronger: level became ambiguous within a single file.* Level 4 is an error outside a =:DATE_TREE:= subtree and *is* the day node inside one, so =bin/lint-org= now carries a *context-sensitive* level rule -- the level number alone stopped being a sufficient predicate. Level 1 likewise now means two things, task category and datetree root; =* Review and planning= holds no tasks directly. This is the first *measured* instance of the overload this heading had only hypothesised.

*And the shipped fix is itself the argument.* Depth could not carry the distinction, so the lint gates on org-datetree's literal title patterns instead -- a property-shaped answer smuggled in as a string match. A =:DATETREE_ROLE:= property would be the honest form. Note where that lands on this heading's own membership table: =:DATE_TREE:= plus pattern-inference is declared *at the container*, which is the drifting shape, not at the member.

*Correction to the work item recorded above.* "=bin/lint-org='s header would need updating from 'category, task, epic-child'" already happened on 2026-08-21, for an unrelated reason -- it now reads "three levels ... outside a =:DATE_TREE:= subtree". So that header is no longer the untouched baseline this note assumed, and the change was made to accommodate a datetree rather than to adopt any tier scheme.

*Promotion trigger, since "parked, not accepted" deserves a condition rather than a mood:* move to TODO when a *second* independent overload of heading level appears. One instance is a special case; two is a pattern, and the second is the case this heading was parked for want of. Related and not duplicates: [[id:9651d4c8-6887-4b0a-91d9-562c3bc49a75][9651d4c8]] is the same membership-by-reference problem arriving from the plan-coverage side, and [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]] is where the flattening half was closed against.

*The promotion trigger above has fired, same day.* Measured 2026-08-21 against =org-archive.el= and a scratch archive run, prompted by the user's observation that flattening the categories away would let archiving produce a chronological record.

*Archiving files by =CLOSED=, not by archive time.* =org-archive-subtree= sets its datetree date to =(or (org-entry-get nil "CLOSED" t) time)=. Verified: four headings archived in one batch at 15:12 landed under =2026-07-14=, =2026-08-03= (two) and =2026-08-20=. A backlog archived late still lands on its real completion dates. Note the ordering is *ascending*, and within a day node entries append in archive order -- so the record is forward-chronological, not reverse; newest-first is a rendering choice, not a storage one.

*Weeks are not reachable by configuration.* =datetree/= is hardwired -- "corresponds to 3 levels of headings" -- and calls =org-datetree-find-date-create=, whose signature is =(d &optional keep-restriction)= with no grouping parameter; the only datetree defcustom is =org-datetree-add-timestamp=. =org-datetree-find-create-hierarchy= does support week grouping, but nothing in the archive path reaches it. A coarser tree needs a custom archive function.

*Org already stores the category as a property.* Archived entries carry =:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY:=, alongside =:ARCHIVE_FILE:=, =:ARCHIVE_TODO:=, =:ARCHIVE_TIME:= and =:ARCHIVE_OLPATH:=. So flattening does not *lose* the category -- org demotes it to exactly the org-native property this heading argues for, and a property is queryable and column-viewable where a heading level is not. That weakens [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]]'s reason for closing the flattening question against.

*And the second overload, which is the promotion evidence.* Archived entries land at *level 4* beneath year/month/day. In TODO.org level 4 means "day node"; in DONE.org it would mean "archived work item", and org's archive tree carries no =:DATE_TREE:= property at all, so [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]'s new lint rule rejects the whole thing. This instance is stronger than the first: *org itself* is overloading level, through its own built-in archive path, rather than a convention this project chose. Depth as the tier carrier is fighting the tool.

*Promoted MAYBE -> TODO on that basis*, by the condition recorded above, six hours after it was written. The story-by-reference composition the flattening enables is filed separately at [[id:8ca6541d-0fc7-45a2-a4d3-76e1608f658d][8ca6541d]] rather than folded in here.

*What this heading is actually for, stated by the user 2026-08-25.* The title says "adopt org-native mechanisms for the heading tiers", which is the *means*. The end is narrower and worth naming: *flatten away the top-level categories and capture them some other way.*

Everything already verified above serves that -- =:CATEGORY:= inherited by a level-3 grandchild, tag groups, both absent from this repo -- but it reads as a menu of org features rather than as one change with a purpose.

*The three tiers resolve into distinct things once flattening happens, and only then.*

| term | after flattening | today |
|-------+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------|
| epic | a *label* -- tag or =:CATEGORY:= -- carried by headings, not a place they live | a level-1 heading, structural |
| story | a real parent heading, free to *cut across* epics because epics are no longer structural | cannot span categories without refiling |
| slice | a *sequenced selection*, composed by reference | no representation at all |

*A slice is the thing that needs composition by reference,* and this is the correction to [[id:8ca6541d-0fc7-45a2-a4d3-76e1608f658d][8ca6541d]]. A slice picks off open tasks *at either level* -- a whole story, or a single task inside one -- and *sequences them intentionally*. That is not a subtree and cannot be: its members already live somewhere, its ordering is the point, and the same task can appear in one slice and not the next. A checkbox list of id links is the natural shape for exactly that, and only that.

*Stories, by contrast, stop needing it.* 8ca6541d proposes by-reference composition *for stories*, and its reasoning is sound *given today's structure*: "a story whose members sit in different categories cannot be expressed without moving a heading across a category boundary". Flattening removes that constraint at the root. So 8ca6541d's premise is *conditional on this heading not happening* -- and if it does, 8ca6541d changes subject rather than becoming wrong.

*Which sequences the two.* This heading is upstream: decide flattening first, and 8ca6541d's scope follows from the answer. Doing 8ca6541d first would build by-reference composition for stories and then discover that stories no longer need it while slices do.

*And it settles the vocabulary,* which is currently loose -- "slice" appears five times in this file across three unrelated senses (a portion of work, a portion of data, and the next-session work queue). Under this model it has exactly one meaning, and it is the third: a sequenced selection of open work. The other two uses are casual and should give way.

*Note the fourth class 8ca6541d found is unaffected and still unresolved:* category, container/epic, datetree node and story are each detected by a different bespoke mechanism, none declared. Flattening removes one of those detectors -- heading level -- which makes the inconsistency smaller but does not answer how a story or a slice declares itself.


** TODO Compose stories by reference: a checkbox list of id links, blocked on its members :code:research:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       8ca6541d-0fc7-45a2-a4d3-76e1608f658d
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 15:26]
:END:

*A story is a fourth heading class, and that -- not the story -- is the finding.* The user's point 2026-08-21: "story" becomes another category, qualified by its members having a particular structure, which deserves its own convention-enforcement definition. Taking it seriously exposes a pattern this project has been repeating without naming.

Four classes exist, each detected by a *different, bespoke* mechanism, and not one of them declared:

| class             | detected by                                  | asserted of it                                          |
|-------------------+----------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------|
| category          | heading *level* (= 1)                        | no =:ID:=, no =:CREATED:=, no keyword, no tags; has =:ARCHIVE:= |
| container / epic  | =--container-heading-p=, a descendant scan   | carries a statistics cookie                             |
| datetree node     | =--datetree-node-role=, position *plus* a title regex | day node carries =:ID:= and =:CREATED:=            |
| story             | -- nothing yet --                            | checkbox list of id links; blocker ids match those links; cookie present |

A level test, a descendant scan, and a position-and-regex test: three answers to one question, and a fourth class would need a fourth. That is a sharper argument for [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] than either overload of heading level -- the problem is not that depth carries too much, it is that there is *no way to say what kind a heading is*, so every class invents its own tell. Verified 2026-08-21: no =:KIND:=, =:TYPE:= or equivalent property exists anywhere in this repo.

*And a story cannot be emergent, which is the substantive break from the container.* =--container-heading-p= can *derive* container-ness because "has a TODO-carrying descendant" is an unambiguous structural fact. "Has a checkbox list of id links" is not -- an ordinary body may hold one for reference, and inferring story-ness from it would misclassify. So the story class must be *declared*. That does not contradict the emergent/declared dichotomy; it is that dichotomy doing its job, and it is why epics stay derived while stories are marked.

*A tag is the likely mechanism, and it is already sanctioned.* =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= holds that tags are free-form beyond the four declared, so =#+TAGS:= gains one word. A tag is also natively agenda-matchable (=+story=), which is exactly what [[id:82df2a6c-881d-4117-a752-5b3b04c9656d][82df2a6c]] exists to evaluate -- so choosing it would put a first real query in front of that heading rather than a hypothetical one. One detail to check rather than assume: =org-use-tag-inheritance= defaults to =t= and is unset in the Doom config, so a story's tag would be inherited by any subheading it ever acquired. Harmless while stories have no children by design, but it is the shape that bites later.

*The enforcement definition belongs in =bin/lint-org=*, beside the category, container-cookie and datetree rules it already carries -- not in [[id:d2a0f54c-b59a-4855-ae3d-c93a6d3aa177][d2a0f54c]]'s proposed =bin/check-conventions=. That split is [[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]'s: check-conventions checks claims *about* the system, =bin/lint-org= checks the org files' *own* structure. A story's qualification is the latter.

*This heading's premise is conditional, and the condition is [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]]* -- established by the user 2026-08-25, and it changes what this is about rather than whether it is right.

The reasoning above is sound *given today's structure*: "a story whose members sit in different categories cannot be expressed without moving a heading across a category boundary". That constraint exists only because categories are *structural* -- a level-1 heading is a place, so belonging to one means living there.

29439196 removes it at the root. Flatten the categories into a label carried by a heading rather than a place it lives, and a *story becomes an ordinary parent heading that can span epics*. It stops needing composition by reference, because refiling stops being the cost.

*What still needs by-reference composition is a slice,* and that is a different object than this heading describes. A slice picks off open work *at either level* -- a whole story, or one task inside one -- and *sequences it intentionally*. Its members already live somewhere, its ordering is the payload, and the same task may appear in one slice and not the next. A checkbox list of id links fits that exactly, and fits a story only as a workaround for structure that 29439196 proposes to remove.

*So the title's "stories" is likely to become "slices"* -- but only after flattening, not now. Asked directly on 2026-08-25 whether the current parlance made it "slices" already, the answer was measured from corpus usage and came back *no*: 33 uses of "story" against 5 loose uses of "slice", with the tiers naming "level 1 epic, level 2 story, level 3". That measurement was accurate about established usage and blind to an intended model being revised -- frequency cannot see where a word is going.

*Sequencing consequence, which is the practical point.* Building this first delivers by-reference composition for stories and then discovers that stories no longer need it while slices do. 29439196 is upstream: decide flattening, and this heading's scope follows from the answer.

*Unaffected:* the fourth-heading-class finding above. Flattening removes one bespoke detector -- heading level -- which makes the inconsistency smaller without answering how a story or a slice declares itself.

Proposed by the user 2026-08-21, as what flattening the categories ([[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]]) would enable. A *story* is a composite task whose members are named *by reference* -- a checkbox list of =[[id:]]= links -- rather than contained as children. No nested copies at a lower level; tasks keep their own subtasks exactly as now.

*Why the archive orders it correctly for free.* Archiving files by =CLOSED= (measured on 29439196), and a story cannot close before its members, so it lands *later* in the datetree than everything it references. The archive then reads as narrative: the constituent work, then the story summarising it. Day-granular only -- within one day node entries append in archive order, not by =CLOSED= time.

*Two halves are already native, verified 2026-08-21:*
- A headline =[/]= cookie *counts body checkboxes*, not just TODO children -- =* TODO A story [/]= over three items became =[1/3]= with no children present. So progress is visible and self-maintaining, and =bin/lint-org='s container-cookie rule is satisfiable.
- =:BLOCKER: ids(...)= already refuses DONE until every named id is finished, via =org-depend=, which this project loads. "Closes only once its checklist completes" therefore needs no new machinery -- it is the blocker list.

*One gap, and it is the whole build.* Nothing ticks a checkbox when the referenced heading closes. =:TRIGGER:= acts on *headings* by id (=XYZ-is-my-id(TODO)=, =chain-siblings=) and has no checkbox facility -- read from =org-depend.el=, not assumed. The seam here is the apply path: every DONE transition already flows through one reviewed command.

*This answers 29439196's own objection to the shape.* That heading's membership table calls a container-declared link list the *drifting* form, "one edit point nobody revisits". Two things change that here. The blocker list is the machine-readable half and the checkbox list the human half, so neither carries the burden alone; and a lint assertion that the two id sets *match* makes disagreement loud rather than silent. =bin/lint-org= already validates that every =:BLOCKER:= resolves and would actually block, so this is one more assertion in an existing harness rather than a new guard.

*Emergent vs declared, reconciled rather than contradicted.* The epic stays emergent -- a subtree that acquired TODO-carrying children, detected by =claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p=. The story is explicitly *declared*. Separating them stops one mechanism serving two masters: today a container is simultaneously "what happened to grow children" and "what you would want to slice by", and those are rarely the same set.

*Consequence for the predicates, which must not be missed.* =--container-heading-p= scans descendants for TODO keywords, so a story holding only checkboxes is *not* a container by that test -- making it eligible for auto-clock-in and for auto-promotion to NEXT, which is precisely the defect [[id:42808717-2736-4bf4-872e-887f990203ca][42808717]] fixed for containers. Any story mechanism has to teach that predicate about stories in the same pass, or it reintroduces a bug closed the same day this was proposed.

*Closely related and not a duplicate:* [[id:9651d4c8-6887-4b0a-91d9-562c3bc49a75][9651d4c8]] asks which of a container's children were in its original plan. A declared story answers that question by construction, since membership is written rather than inferred from what accumulated.

- [2026-08-21 Fri 14:53]--[2026-08-21 Fri 15:42] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 15:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 16:02] =>  0:03
- [2026-08-21 Fri 15:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 16:02] assigned at review
:END:

The missing half of [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]], which is entirely cost analysis and mechanism comparison with no statement of what the change would *buy*. That asymmetry is the likeliest reason it has sat parked since 2026-08-19 instead of resolving either way.

*Why the features are unused, stated by the user 2026-08-21, and it is not neglect.* It is this project's founding motivation arriving one level up. CLAUDE.md's opening goal is "natural-language manipulation of =.org= files from within Emacs ... without needing to internalise Emacs chord sequences" -- and judging an agenda view, a sparse tree or a column view currently *requires* exactly that fluency, because you cannot evaluate a feature you cannot yet invoke. Nor was there, until this project, a corpus over which exercising them would show anything.

*The corpus now exists, measured 2026-08-21:* TODO.org 8433 lines across 8 categories; DONE.org 9554 lines and 63 archived entries; 353 =[[id:]]= links between the live documents; roughly 200 headings carrying nine TODO keywords and four tags; a =:DATE_TREE:= subtree; and clock data on most active headings.

*Advertised features to point at it, all read-only:*
- =org-agenda= tag/property/TODO matching across =org-agenda-files=, with =org-agenda-custom-commands= to save composite views
- sparse trees (=org-match-sparse-tree=, =C-c /=), which fold away non-matches in the buffer itself
- column view (=org-columns= plus a =COLUMNS= property) -- properties as a table, with summary roll-ups
- =:CATEGORY:= as an inherited agenda label, and statistics cookies: both already verified working here by 29439196, and both unused
- =org-ql=, already a dependency -- =org_query= wraps it, so its query language is reachable from a session today

*The decisive reframing: none of this requires the refactor.* Agenda views, sparse trees and column view all run against the structure exactly as it stands. So 29439196's implied sequencing -- settle the tier mechanism, then get the views -- is backwards. Look through org's own lenses at the corpus first. The views either show something worth having or they do not, and only then is there a real question about whether tiers must move to get more of it.

*What it would buy if they do earn it.* A story or slice becomes a *query* rather than a subtree, composable without relocating headings -- which is the shared problem behind 29439196's membership table and [[id:9651d4c8-6887-4b0a-91d9-562c3bc49a75][9651d4c8]]'s plan-coverage question. Both would largely dissolve.

*And Claude is how they get evaluated without the chords*, which closes the loop: a view or column spec can be built, run and rendered on request, so muscle memory stops being a precondition for the judgement. That is the founding goal applied to org's own feature set rather than to task edits.

Observed 2026-08-19: an apply pass refused
[[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]]'s =none -> TODO=
because =--at-id= could not find the heading. The heading existed; the
index did not know where.

*Cause.* It was captured with no target, so
[[id:97696fc2-f07b-4586-b280-de6a7d26fb87][97696fc2]] put it in
=~/org/notes.org=, and it was then moved into =TODO.org= by cutting and
re-inserting the subtree text. That never touches =org-id-locations=, so
=org-id-find= kept resolving to a file the heading had left.

*Wider than its cause.* Any hand move between files does this — a
=git= operation, an =Edit= tool call, a refile done as text. Only
=org-refile= maintains the index, and this project edits org text
directly all the time.

*Candidate fixes, none chosen.* Call =org-id-update-id-locations= after
any tool that relocates a subtree; or have =--at-id= fall back to a scan
of the tracked files when the index misses, which is self-healing and
costs a scan only on the failing path. The second also covers moves made
outside Emacs entirely, which the first cannot.

*Left as a question:* whether the =Error:= that reaches the review
buffer should distinguish "no such :ID: anywhere" from "not where the
index says", since the two want different responses and currently read
identically.


** DONE A no-op TODO -> TODO transition should never be queued :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-20 Thu 07:38]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       cc0c17a7-a554-426d-afeb-09cbee7339f0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-19 Wed 19:24]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 22:24]--[2026-08-19 Wed 22:29] =>  0:05
- [2026-08-19 Wed 22:24]--[2026-08-19 Wed 22:29] assigned at review
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 22:25] \\
  refuse no-op transitions at the tool
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 07:33]--[2026-08-20 Thu 07:39] =>  0:06
- [2026-08-20 Thu 07:33]--[2026-08-20 Thu 07:39] assigned at review
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-20 Thu 07:38] \\
  refusal shipped, tolerance pinned, premise corrected
CLOCK: [2026-08-22 Sat 09:53]--[2026-08-22 Sat 09:54] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-22 Sat 09:53]--[2026-08-22 Sat 09:54] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-22 Sat 16:37]--[2026-08-22 Sat 16:38] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-22 Sat 16:37]--[2026-08-22 Sat 16:38] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-22 Sat 16:54]--[2026-08-22 Sat 16:58] =>  0:04
- [2026-08-22 Sat 16:54]--[2026-08-22 Sat 16:58] assigned at review
:END:
:PLAN:
Observed 2026-08-19: apply refused [[id:9651d4c8-6887-4b0a-91d9-562c3bc49a75][9651d4c8]]'s =TODO -> TODO=. There was nothing to do -- the heading already held that keyword -- but the event was queued, offered at review, marked, and failed there.

*Cause.* The heading was created by writing the keyword straight into the heading text, and =org_set_todo TODO= was then called on it. The tool recorded the transition faithfully; the reply even said =(was TODO)=, which was the signal and was not read.

*Two places it could be stopped, and they are not equivalent.* =org_set_todo= could refuse to queue when =from= equals =to=, which costs one comparison and gives the caller immediate feedback instead of a failure hours later at apply. Or apply could treat a no-op as satisfied rather than failed, since the file already holds the requested state. Doing both is defensible: refuse early, be tolerant late for events queued before the refusal existed.
:END:

*Problem.* =org_set_todo= queued transitions whose requested state was already the state on disk. Each no-op cost a queue event, a review-buffer line and a dismissal. Four occurrences in one day, all the same pattern: writing a keyword inline at heading creation -- because =org_capture= cannot target a category ([[id:97696fc2-f07b-4586-b280-de6a7d26fb87][97696fc2]]) -- then calling =org_set_todo= for that same state out of habit.

*Solution: refuse at the source; the apply side already tolerated it.* =org_set_todo= now returns an =Error:= reply when the requested state is already held on disk, so =bin/hooks/queue-append= drops the event and the no-op never reaches the queue (=c2cf203=). The =Error:= prefix is the mechanism rather than a verdict -- the hook decides by testing for it, so refusing *requires* it, and the wording carries what the prefix misstates. It compares against disk, not against pending events, which is what =from= means everywhere else. Apply needed no code change, but its tolerance is now pinned by a test rather than working by omission (=9b4d4f8=).

*Verified* end to end through the MCP tool rather than by calling the elisp -- the reply was the refusal and the queue file stayed at 121 lines. An earlier check proved nothing: invoking the function over =emacsclient= never fires the =PostToolUse= hook that writes the queue, so the count could not have moved either way.

*Falsified -- the premise this heading was filed on.* Its original title asserted such a transition "reaches apply and fails there". Probed 2026-08-20: applying a =TODO -> TODO= item returns nil, leaves the keyword untouched and writes no State line, and a queued chain (=NEXT -> DOING= then =NEXT -> DONE=, both carrying =from: NEXT=) applies in one pass. The heading was filed on an inference drawn from two failures in one apply run whose error text was never read. Retitled, because a false title is the worst stale claim: it is what a reader sees without opening the body.

*Loose end, deliberately unexplained:* what actually failed for [[id:9651d4c8-6887-4b0a-91d9-562c3bc49a75][9651d4c8]] in that run. If it fails again, the error text is the evidence this heading never had.

/This heading is the trial vehicle for the/ =:PLAN:= /drawer layout (2026-08-21), which is why its shape differs from its siblings. The planning content sits in a drawer beside/ =:PROPERTIES:= /and/ =:LOGBOOK:= /rather than below the prose, so a folded heading shows the debrief and nothing else./

:END:

  seven of thirteen tools never called

- [2026-08-20 Thu 07:47]--[2026-08-20 Thu 07:52] assigned at review
- State "TODO"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-20 Thu 07:49] \\
  re-queued; keyword was dismissed
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 07:57]--[2026-08-20 Thu 07:58] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-20 Thu 07:57]--[2026-08-20 Thu 07:58] assigned at review
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-20 Thu 21:10] \\
  measuring usage across 69 transcripts
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 07:49]--[2026-08-21 Fri 07:56] =>  0:07
- [2026-08-21 Fri 07:49]--[2026-08-21 Fri 07:56] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 07:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:02] =>  0:03
- [2026-08-21 Fri 07:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:02] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:43]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:45] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-21 Fri 08:43]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:45] assigned at review
:END:

Thirteen tools are advertised. Over a two-day session that did almost
nothing *but* org work, *seven were never called once*: =org_query=,
=org_refile=, =org_move_sibling=, =org_sort_children=,
=org_clock_report=, =org_archive=, =org_log_background_plan=.

*And the work they exist for was done anyway, by other means.* That is
the finding — not that they went unneeded, but that they went unreached:

| what happened                                    | tool not reached for |
|--------------------------------------------------+----------------------|
| cross-file searches, dozens of them              | =org_query=          |
| moved three subtrees between files and levels    | =org_refile=         |
| ordered a composed epic's children               | =org_sort_children=  |
| totalled clock time over the whole corpus        | =org_clock_report=   |

Each was done with =grep=, =python=, or hand-written =org-cut-subtree=
/=org-paste-subtree= elisp. In every case the tool would have been
shorter and safer; the hand-rolled subtree move is exactly what left
=org-id= stale in
[[id:8ddd7fa8-e847-42e9-b82c-63bbe4c11b7b][8ddd7fa8]].

*Four specific mis-advertisements, each with a cost already paid:*

1. =org_capture='s =target= is optional and omitting it writes *outside
   the repository*, to the capture file. The description does not say
   so. Cost: a heading filed where the user could not see it
   \([[id:97696fc2-f07b-4586-b280-de6a7d26fb87][97696fc2]]\).
2. =org_capture= can only target an =:ID:= or a top-level heading *of
   the capture file*, so filing under a real category is impossible —
   categories deliberately carry no =:ID:=, and =bin/lint-org= enforces
   that. The natural call cannot be expressed, so headings get
   hand-written instead.
3. =org_amend= only ever appends. Nothing says so at the call site, and
   it is the mechanism behind
   [[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]]: every summary
   and correction lands furthest from where a reader starts.
4. =org_set_todo= accepted a transition to the state already held, four
   times in one day, before =c2cf203= refused it.

*The question this heading asks* is not "are the descriptions accurate"
— mostly they are, and several are excellent. It is whether they are
written so a model reaches for them *at the moment of need*. A
description read once at session start competes with =grep=, which needs
no recall at all. What would change that is unproven: naming the
alternative it beats, stating the failure it prevents, or something
else.

*Measurement to repeat, since it is cheap:* the queue's =source= field
records every tool that queues, and this session shows =org_set_todo= 36,
=org_clock_out= 10, =org_clock_in= 9. The immediate tools leave no trace
at all, which is itself worth fixing before judging them — right now
"never used" and "used and forgotten" are indistinguishable for seven of
the thirteen.

*Measured 2026-08-20 across all 69 session transcripts in this project.* The measurement half of this heading is now done; what remains is the child and whatever follows from finding 4.

*1. The original premise held for a session and does not hold for the corpus.* Only *one* tool of fourteen has never been called in any session: =org_sort_children=. =org_query=, =org_refile= and =org_move_sibling=, named above as never called, have since been used -- 27, 14 and 2 times. The "seven of thirteen" observation was true of the two-day session it was made in, and that scoping is what makes it interesting rather than wrong.

*2. Per-session breadth is the real number, and it is low.* Of 20 sessions making 10 or more org tool calls, the median reaches for *5 distinct tools of 14*. No session used more than 8. So the surface is not unused; it is unused *at any given moment*.

*3. =org_amend= is the sharpest case of recall rather than utility.* 121 lifetime calls -- the third most-used tool -- yet absent from 16 of 20 active sessions. When it is remembered it carries a session; most sessions never think of it. Also absent from most active sessions: =org_log_background_plan= (19/20), =org_archive= (18/20), =org_refile= (18/20), =org_move_sibling= (18/20), =org_clock_report= (17/20).

*4. The finding that reframes this heading: a standing rule works and a good description does not.* CLAUDE.md requires calling =org_pending_updates= before the first state or clock call of a session. Compliance since that rule landed on 2026-08-17 is *4 of 4 sessions, 100%*; before it, 3 of 34, and those three were coincidence. Small n and worth re-measuring, but unambiguous so far.

Set against it, on the same day: =org_capture='s description states in capital letters that it writes NO TODO keyword and that =org_set_todo= must follow. That instruction was ignored five times in one session by a reader who had the description in context, losing four heading bodies ([[id:1cd2df62-35e9-47dc-bcce-dda7bae3c522][1cd2df62]]).

*So the title's framing may be wrong.* This heading asks how the tools are *advertised*. The evidence says advertising is not the lever that moves usage -- a directive in CLAUDE.md is, because it is read at session start rather than at the moment of a call that is never made. If under-use is worth fixing, the intervention to test is a standing rule naming the tool for a situation, not a better =:description=.

*What not to conclude.* Low breadth is not obviously a defect. Sorting a heading's children is genuinely rare, and =org_sort_children='s zero calls may be honest rather than a discovery failure. Deciding which of the six under-used tools deserve a rule, and which are simply niche, is the remaining judgement and is not answered by any of the above.

=org_capture= takes =(title target tags note)=. On 2026-08-20 it was called five times with two invented arguments, =todo_state= and =body=. Every call succeeded, returned /"Captured: ... (ID: ...)"/, and silently dropped both.

*The measured damage, all in one session.* Three headings landed with no TODO keyword. *Four landed with no body at all* -- =41cf5830=, =c6fc6f46=, =5a5e87c9= and this one -- losing every word of analysis written into them. Only =a729c32a= survived, and only because =org_amend= was called on it later for an unrelated reason.

*Neither loss announced itself.* A keywordless heading is absent from every agenda TODO view, invisible to =org_query todo:TODO=, uncounted by its parent's statistics cookie, and ignored by =--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo=. An empty body looks exactly like a heading someone has not written up yet. Both were reported to the user as filed work, with content quoted back that was never in the file.

*It went unnoticed for five hours and was found by accident* -- the user asked where a concept had been parked, and the heading holding it was empty. Nothing in the tool's replies, the lint run, the test suite or the review queue could have surfaced it, because from every one of those angles a heading with a title and an =:ID:= is well-formed.

*The tool was not at fault and this is the point.* Its description says, in capitals, that the heading is written with /NO TODO keyword/ and that the state must be set afterwards with =org_set_todo=; it advertises no =body= parameter at all. The documentation was correct, prominent, and unread. Only the silence made the error survivable. A parameter the schema does not declare should be an error, not a no-op -- the same argument =org_set_todo= already accepts for an undeclared keyword, which returns /"WAIT is not a TODO keyword in this file (have: ...)"/ rather than proceeding.

*Where the check belongs is the open question.* If =claude-code-ide-mcp= dispatches by arity or by plist, an unknown key may be discarded before this module ever sees it, in which case this is an upstream report ([[id:9d6449c7-529f-49a0-b28f-015cce869715][9d6449c7]]) rather than a local fix. Establish which before proposing anything.

*Related defect found in the same reading:* =org_capture='s own description contained a truncated sentence -- /"Run org_outline first if you need to see what "/ ran straight into /"Returns a confirmation containing the "/, losing "categories exist." Repaired 2026-08-20. Evidence for this parent's premise that the advertising has never been read end to end.

/Body restored 2026-08-20 by =org_amend=; the original capture silently discarded it -- by the very defect this heading describes./

*Gating question answered 2026-08-20: the drop is upstream, so this is a report, not a local fix.* =claude-code-ide-mcp-http-server--validate-args= iterates over =arg-specs= -- the declared schema -- and pulls each declared name out of the supplied =args= alist. It never iterates =args=. A key the schema does not declare is therefore never looked at, and silence is structural rather than a missing check:

: (dolist (spec arg-specs (nreverse result))
:   (let* ((name (plist-get spec :name))
:          (optional (plist-get spec :optional))
:          (value (alist-get (intern name) args)))
:     (when (and (not optional) (not value))
:       (signal 'json-rpc-error (list -32602 (format "Missing required argument: %s" name))))
:     (push value result)))

Note the asymmetry: a *missing required* argument signals =-32602=, so the machinery for refusing a malformed call already exists and is used. Only the unknown-key direction is unguarded. That makes the upstream report a small one -- one pass over =args= for keys absent from =arg-specs= -- rather than a design change. Belongs with [[id:9d6449c7-529f-49a0-b28f-015cce869715][9d6449c7]] alongside the =200=-versus-=202= finding.

There is a second dispatch path, =claude-code-ide-mcp.el= line 352, which hands the whole alist to a handler; it was not examined. Establish whether it has the same gap before writing the report.

*The same reading turned up a live trap in this project's own schema, now fixed.* Keys are matched verbatim -- =(intern name)= against the JSON key -- so the advertised spelling is what a caller must send. =org_sort_children= advertised =sort-type= with a hyphen while every other multi-word argument in all fourteen tools uses snake_case. A model sending the conventional =sort_type= would have had it silently discarded, which is this heading's failure mode arriving by a different door. Renamed to =sort_type= 2026-08-20.

*The rest of the surface is clean, which is worth recording so it is not re-audited.* All fourteen tools were checked mechanically, comparing each advertised argument name against its handler's arglist: *zero mismatches* once snake_case is normalised to kebab-case. Every advertised parameter exists and every real parameter is advertised. The five failures on 2026-08-20 -- =todo_state=, =body=, =file=, =max_level=, and =body= again -- were invented wholesale, not misread from a bad schema. Fixing descriptions would not have prevented any of them.

*One measured consequence beyond the lost bodies:* an =org_outline= call passing =file= and =max_level= returned the *unrestricted default* rather than the scoped view it appeared to return, and was reported as scoped. A dropped argument does not merely fail to apply -- it silently substitutes a different answer to a different question.

=org_amend= appends to the end of a heading's body and can do nothing
else. Its description says so — "the text lands at the end of the
heading's own body" — but frames it as *placement*, not as a
constraint, and nothing at the call site suggests what it costs.

*What it costs.* Every correction, every outcome summary, every "this
turned out to be wrong" lands furthest from where a reader starts. That
is the mechanism behind
[[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]]: 226ed53b reached
274 lines with its summary nowhere, because the only tool available put
each addition at the bottom. Writing that summary where it belonged took
a hand edit through =emacsclient=, and the first attempt corrupted a
sibling heading.

*The shape of the gap*, stated so it is not confused with a formatting
preference: a journal genuinely accumulates and appending is right for
it. What has no tool at all is *revising* — correcting a claim in place,
or putting a summary first. So the question is not whether =org_amend=
should insert elsewhere, but whether a second operation is wanted beside
it.

*Options, none chosen:*
- A =position= argument on =org_amend= — cheapest, and the least
  honest, since "append or prepend" is not the distinction that matters.
- A separate tool for *replacing* a delimited region, which is what a
  correction actually is.
- Neither: keep appending and let the closing action fold history into a
  drawer, which is the direction
  [[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]] was heading
  before it was gated on the task lifecycle.

Filed 2026-08-20 as the one mis-advertisement from
[[id:420816ec-677e-45a5-b898-fb5db70d214b][420816ec]] with no home of its
own. It is also the capture that proved
[[id:bd482c92-a71a-4d7d-88df-47678ee94c79][bd482c92]] fixed: this heading
was filed with =target: "Skill logic"=, a category title, which was
refused outright the day before.

*Decided 2026-08-20 by the user: =org_amend= should allow wholesale revision of a body, not only append.* This closes the three-option question above by rejecting its premise — the problem is not where a correction lands, it is that append-only forces every body to be a transcript.

The objection that append-only protected against was losing the evolution of a body. It does not need protecting: *git already holds every revision*, so the discipline is simply to commit before revising. That makes revision cheap and reversible, and it is the same argument =plans/= already rests on.

*What an open heading's body is for, stated positively:* the problem, the latest intended approach, and relevant verified facts. Not the stream of consciousness of how we got there. The body describes the current state of the question, not its history.

*What a closed heading's body is for:* a concise debrief — what the problem was (a slightly elaborated restatement of the title) and what the actual solution turned out to be. The original problem statement and plan of attack move into a drawer rather than being deleted.

*Two additions offered against the user's \"keep it short\" constraint, both already in DONE.org's house style:* how it was verified, in a clause; and any claim that was *falsified*, because that is what stops the same wrong turn being taken twice. This project's own evidence supports the second — three headings carried confident design claims later found wrong, while no journal claim needed correcting in the same period. Four short items total, not four paragraphs.

*Under consideration, not decided:* a timestamped commit log in a drawer, giving a reader an index into the git history for the rare \"how did we get here?\" question, so that the body never has to carry that answer itself.

This heading is now a *dependency of* [[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]] rather than a neighbour of it: the retention policy cannot be applied to an existing body without a tool that can rewrite one.

*Filed 2026-08-20, not built. An interim convention landed 2026-08-21 and does not close this.*

*A workaround now exists in =.claude/skills/org/SKILL.md= ("Body prose and the task lifecycle").* It cannot put the outcome first -- append-only forbids that -- but it stops the body *contradicting* the keyword, which is the half that was actually costing time. The rule: the keyword owns the status, completion vocabulary (=Shipped=, =Fixed=, =Verified by mutation=) is reserved for outcome prose, and investigation words (=Measured=, =Observed=, =Reproduced=) are what a capture may use.

*The word that broke it was =Verified=,* which fits both stages. Written on 2026-08-21 in two opposite senses within one hour: =Verified on org 9.6.15= (the investigation is sound, heading still TODO) and =Verified by mutation= (the fix works, heading DONE). Nothing distinguished them, and the user reported mistaking filed headings for resolved ones the same day.

*Measured, since the cost had only been asserted:* TODO.org is ~118,000 tokens. Across 72 active headings the median body is *50 lines*, the longest 404, and *44 bodies of 40+ lines carry 86% of all active body text*. That is the context every session re-reads to understand a heading.

*The deletion objection is weaker than this heading's neighbourhood assumed.* CLAUDE.md calls deletion "the one irreversible half of the practice", but justifies it by =plans/='s history being *bounded*. That justification does not transfer to =.org= files, which are version-controlled in full -- as the user pointed out, superseded prose stays recoverable from the commit history. Revising in place is therefore reversible here, and the case against building this tool is correspondingly thinner. The residual risk is only prose written and deleted inside one uncommitted window.

*It blocks [[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]]*, which is the user's standing concern -- raised 2026-08-18 as "I am questioning the long-term utility of capturing this quantity of minutiae", and repeated 2026-08-21 with the added observation that the accumulated prose is costing *working context*, not just readability. Machinery gates the policy: what to stop capturing is hard to settle while nothing can revise what was already captured.

*The cheapest mitigation needs neither this tool nor that decision:* write shorter bodies. That is standing user guidance -- economise on body prose, never on heading count -- and 2026-08-21's own output did not follow it.

*The policy was applied by hand to one closed body, 2026-08-21* ([[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::f0cdc90][f0cdc90]]). [[id:cc0c17a7-a554-426d-afeb-09cbee7339f0][cc0c17a7]] went from 138 lines to 34: the four items, one live loose end, and the original problem statement plus plan of attack moved into a drawer. It was a good test case because its body *contradicted itself* -- "Still open: the apply-side half" forty lines above "The apply-side half needed nothing, because the premise was wrong" -- so a reader stopping in the middle came away with a false belief. That is the append-only defect in its most expensive form.

*So the answer to "can this be done programmatically" is yes, and the answer to "should it be done this way" is no.* Locating a heading by =:ID:=, taking the range to the next heading and rewriting it is a dozen lines of script. The hazard is not the rewrite, it is the range.

*The first attempt corrupted the file, and this is the second recorded instance.* The next-heading test was =startswith("*")=, which matched a *bold prose line* (=*Cause.*=) rather than a headline, so it replaced 6 lines of a 123-line body and left the remainder orphaned below the new text. The body above already records that an earlier hand edit through =emacsclient= "corrupted a sibling heading". Two corruptions, two different range bugs, both from hand-rolling the same operation -- which is the argument for a tool that owns the range logic once, rather than for care.

*And =bin/lint-org= did not catch it: 0 errors on the corrupted file.* The lint checks structure, and the corruption was prose-level -- orphaned paragraphs under an otherwise well-formed heading. Nothing mechanical would have caught it. *Git was the only safety net*, and =git checkout -- TODO.org= restored all 136 lines exactly. That converts "commit before revising" from ceremony into the load-bearing precondition, and it is the concrete vindication of the deletion-reversibility argument recorded above.

*The drawer is =:PLAN:=, chosen by the user 2026-08-21.* The trial first used =:ORIGINAL:=, which I coined on the spot from the policy's own wording -- not from any source, and without grepping first, which is the search-before-coining rule failing in the one place it was most obviously due. =:PLAN:= is the project's existing word (=plans/=, Plan Mode, the Plan link, =org_log_background_plan=) and was free: zero prior occurrences in the repo. It belongs in =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md=, and =bin/lint-org= can then assert that a closed heading's planning content lives in it.

*Immediate consequence worth sequencing on:* this operation is what [[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]] is blocked on. Archiving stalls because CLAUDE.md requires an outcome summary before a =DONE= heading is archived, and there are 30 of them; producing those summaries *is* this revision applied 30 times.

*Shipped 2026-08-24, the wrapping half only* (=8170ddd=). =org_wrap_plan= wraps a body's prospective part in a =:PLAN:= drawer, with an optional =until= marker naming the first line that stays in the body -- the retroactive case, where a body written before the convention already holds both halves.

Two insertions at computable positions, never a range replacement, and no boundary comes from a regexp of ours: =outline-next-heading= and =org-end-of-meta-data= decide, so a line org would not parse as a heading cannot be treated as one. *Verified by reintroducing the bug*: swapping in the =startswith("*")= next-heading test that orphaned 117 lines on 2026-08-21 fails exactly two of the five new tests and nothing else. Refusals rather than guesses on a missing, duplicated or first-line seam, because a silently-wrong seam is the one mistake here no later reader will catch -- the drawer is one readers are told to skip.

*This heading is not done.* Its title names the real problem -- =org_amend= can still only append -- and general in-place revision, which *continuous paring* needs, is untouched. What shipped is the separable half the completion transition needs, split out deliberately so [[id:8bcd56f4-22a8-4673-bad6-a99433027b81][8bcd56f4]] and [[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]] stop waiting on the harder one.

Designed by the user 2026-08-21/22. A heading body has two lives, and today they are the same text in the same place, which is why bodies become transcripts.

*Prospective, while the task is open.* The body holds motivation, observations and speculation -- everything leading up to the moment of implementation. It is *pared and revised continuously*, from creation right up to realization, so that it stays concise and internally consistent. It describes the current state of the question, never the history of how the question moved. The plan-file link, when one exists, goes at the *end* of the body the moment it is composed: the plan can supersede any statement above it, so it must be read last.

*Retrospective, once the task is carried out.* The whole prospective body -- plan link included -- is wrapped in a =:PLAN:= drawer, which sits beside =:PROPERTIES:= and =:LOGBOOK:= rather than below the prose. The body is then the debrief alone: problem restated, what the solution turned out to be, how it was verified, what was falsified ([[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]]'s four items). A folded heading therefore shows the debrief and nothing else.

*Relocation, never deletion.* The plan link moves into the drawer with the rest of the planning content because it *is* planning content -- and a forward-looking pointer inside a retrospective readout invites a reader to treat the plan as current. CLAUDE.md's "not removed at =DONE=" is a rule against losing the link, not against moving it, and must be reworded to say so.

*Readers skip the drawer.* The org skill should tell agentic readers to ignore =:PLAN:= entirely until a retrospective "how did we get here" question is actually asked. The debrief and the source code describe present reality; the plan describes an intention that may not have survived contact.

*Two mechanisms, and only one of them is missing.* Measured 2026-08-21 on [[id:cc0c17a7-a554-426d-afeb-09cbee7339f0][cc0c17a7]]: =org_amend= already appends *below* a =:PLAN:= drawer that sits at the top of a body, so the debrief needs no new tool at all. The only new operation is *wrapping* -- insert the drawer at the end-of-drawers boundary, close it after the last body line -- which is two insertions at computable positions rather than a range replacement. That distinction is not cosmetic: both of that day's file corruptions were range bugs, and neither was caught by =bin/lint-org=, which sees structure and not prose. Continuous paring still needs general in-place revision, which is why this is blocked on 3063c3e5 rather than merely related to it.

*What it enables mechanically:* =bin/lint-org= can assert that a =DONE= heading with a substantial body carries a =:PLAN:= drawer -- the project's usual answer to a convention that would otherwise recur ([[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]'s split puts this in the lint, not in =bin/check-conventions=).

*Deliberately deferred until the wrapping tool exists*, so that a rule never describes something no session can perform: CLAUDE.md's plan-link wording, and the org skill's "Body prose and the task lifecycle" section, which is explicitly an interim workaround for append-only ("Until that is fixed") and is superseded wholesale by this. =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= gains =:PLAN:= beside the other drawer conventions in the same pass.

*Migration of the existing backlog is settled and is not this heading's work:* sweep old bodies in unedited, recorded on [[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]]. Old drawers will be raw transcripts where new ones are pared plans; the ignore-the-drawer rule makes that invisible in normal reading.

:END:
*Adopted 2026-08-24* (=44b7020=, =fe376db=). All five deferred edits landed, once [[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]]'s wrapping half made them describable without describing something no session could perform.

1. *CLAUDE.md's plan-link wording* now says the link is *relocated into* =:PLAN:=, not deleted, at =DONE=. The old "not removed at =DONE=" was a rule against *losing* the link and was being read as a rule against *moving* it.
2. *=.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= gains a =:PLAN:= section* beside the other drawer conventions: what goes in it, that =org_wrap_plan= makes it, and that readers skip it.
3. *The org skill's "Body prose and the task lifecycle" section is reframed* -- and this is where the plan and the outcome diverge, deliberately. This heading anticipated the section being "superseded wholesale". It is not, and saying so would have thrown away good rules: the completion-vocabulary table, the date-stamping rule and the brevity rule each stand on their own and are kept. What is superseded is the *framing* -- the section opened by stating that =org_amend= appends and can do nothing else, so an outcome always lands furthest from the reader, and cast everything below it as interim "until that is fixed". That premise is now half-false. The date-stamp rule's stated justification is adjusted too, since it leaned on nothing later needing revision.
4. *The ignore-the-drawer rule is in both files*: treat =:PLAN:= as absent unless the question is explicitly retrospective. Reading it for current fact is how a superseded design claim gets repeated as though it still held -- which is the reason the two halves were separated at all.
5. *=bin/lint-org= asserts it*, and this was the one that needed measuring first.

*The lint rule's scoping is the whole of whether it is usable.* Measured before writing it: unscoped it fires on *124* headings at once -- 30 in TODO.org, 94 in DONE.org -- taking the report from 24 warnings to 147. That is exactly the drowning [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]] had to rescue this lint from once already. Scoped by =CLOSED:= date to headings finished on or after the convention landed, it fires *zero* times today and grows only with new work.

*And that scoping is only possible because of a few hours earlier.* [[id:f4b07fc0-187a-4975-aa3b-b9bd4b1c0799][f4b07fc0]] backfilled 58 =CLOCK=-derived =CLOSED:= markers this morning; before that, "closed after date X" was not a question these files could answer. The backfill was filed as housekeeping and turned out to be the enabling step.

*Because the rule reports nothing on the real files by design, the positive case is a fixture* -- a rule never seen to fail proves nothing. Removing the date scoping fails the exemption test and takes the real report to 147 warnings, so both the rule and its scoping are pinned. =--lint-substantial-body-p= excludes drawer contents, so thirty =CLOCK= lines cannot make a short heading look substantial.

*Still open, and not this heading's:* the backlog itself. 124 finished headings carry a substantial body and no drawer, which is [[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]]'s sweep. The lint is deliberately silent about them rather than counting them daily.

*=:BLOCKER:= dropped 2026-08-24*, having read =ids(3063c3e5)=. It was set because adoption could not describe an operation no session could perform; =org_wrap_plan= shipped this morning, so the thing it guarded exists. [[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]] stays open for the *revision* half its title names, which adoption never needed -- that half serves continuous paring, not the completion transition.

Left in place it would have blocked this heading's own =DONE= at apply: =org-depend-block-todo= refuses a =DONE= whose =:BLOCKER:= names a heading still carrying a keyword, and 3063c3e5 is =NEXT=. So the property had gone from a true statement to a false one that would fire as a real refusal.


** DONE Rename the WAIT keyword to WAITING, matching the org community's spelling :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:20]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       a729c32a-d6b2-49c5-a40e-81ed5adeeb2f
:CREATED:  [2026-08-20 Thu 14:18]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:16]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:20] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from              [2026-08-20 Thu 16:16] \\
  precondition re-verified: 0 WAIT headings, 0 pending WAIT events
- [2026-08-20 Thu 16:16]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:20]
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-20 Thu 16:20] \\
  renamed; hook scope fixed as a prerequisite
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:42]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:46] =>  0:04
- [2026-08-20 Thu 16:42]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:46] assigned at review
:END:

*Done 2026-08-20, same day it was filed.* Both preconditions re-verified immediately before the edit rather than trusted from the survey: zero =WAIT= headings across TODO.org, DONE.org and notes.org, and no unapplied =WAIT= event queued.

*Renamed* where the text states a rule or is a fixture -- both live =#+TODO:= lines plus 73 occurrences across nine files. *Not renamed*, 68 occurrences: every =State "WAIT"= line in a =:LOGBOOK:= drawer, all other TODO.org/DONE.org body prose, and =plans/=.

*The one judgement call:* [[id:241a4c54-ed59-4780-b602-2a86efee978a][241a4c54]]'s body was renamed (4 lines) despite being journal prose, because it holds the four container rules as settled and pending transcription -- [[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]]'s sweep copies them into CLAUDE.md, so leaving =WAIT= there would have seeded the old keyword back into the file this change just cleaned.

*Verified, not assumed.* Both buffers reverted *without* =preserve-modes=, since =#+TODO:= is parsed only at mode init -- the reason the earlier =#+STARTUP: content= check appeared to fail. Both then reported =org-todo-keywords-1= containing =WAITING= and not =WAIT=. =config.el= reloaded into the running Emacs, its literal =(equal state "WAIT")= comparison having been live. Then probed the real tool: =org_set_todo= with =WAIT= returns /"WAIT is not a TODO keyword in this file (have: ... WAITING ...)"/ and queues nothing, so the safety property is demonstrated rather than asserted.

*Two things the survey got wrong.* Scope was estimated at 65 occurrences across seven files; it was 73 across nine, because =bin/queue-append-test= and =bin/pre-commit-test= carry keyword fixtures and the survey's grep was scoped to =*.el=, =*.md=, =*.org= and =*.json= -- extensionless scripts fell outside it. Worth remembering: a file-glob survey under-reports in a repo whose scripts have no extension.

*And the commit was refused, correctly.* The pre-commit hook linted =clock-template.org= -- staged for the first time ever by this change -- against task-file conventions it was never meant to satisfy, producing six false errors. =bin/lint-org= governs exactly TODO.org and DONE.org; the hook gated every staged =.org=. Fixed by scoping the hook to the linter's own set rather than passing =--no-verify=, in its own commit ahead of this one, with a test that fails when the scoping is reverted. A gate wider than its linter blocks correct work and teaches the bypass habit the hook exists to make unnecessary.


* Clock lifecycle & visibility
:PROPERTIES:
:ARCHIVE:  DONE.org::* Clock lifecycle & visibility
:END:

Everything here is specifically about the clock subsystem's
correctness and observability, as opposed to the general TODO-state
machine above — three different angles on "make sure the clock is
never silently wrong."


** TODO A slice gets clocked and auto-promoted, because its members are links not children :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       95c27fca-136c-46f6-b454-fe94261e0e5d
:CREATED:  [2026-08-25 Tue 11:10]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-25 Tue 11:10] \\
  measured against the first real slice
:END:

*Measured 2026-08-25 against the first slice* ([[id:c44c2119-dbf8-4510-99e2-6aaa7f259d94][c44c2119]]), not reasoned about: =(:container-p nil :auto-clock-in t :would-clock t)=. Setting =DOING= on a slice by hand opens a clock on the slice itself.

*The cause is structural and will not go away on its own.* =claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p= asks whether a heading has a descendant carrying a TODO keyword, and exempts containers from =--trigger-auto-clock-in= on the argument that org already rolls a subtree's time up to its parent. A slice's members are =[[id:...]]= links in a checkbox list, not descendants, so the predicate says =nil= and the exemption does not apply -- while the *reason* for the exemption applies with full force, since every referent carries its own clock. The predicate tests the mechanism, and the argument is about the meaning.

*Same shape, second consequence:* =--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo= also refuses containers, and also will not recognise a slice. A sole =TODO= slice under =* Slices= would be promoted to =NEXT= as though it were an action. This is the reintroduction of [[id:42808717-2736-4bf4-872e-887f990203ca][42808717]] by a route its fix did not cover, which [[id:8ca6541d-0fc7-45a2-a4d3-76e1608f658d][8ca6541d]] predicted in the abstract before any slice existed.

*Not urgent today only because the first slice's =DOING= was written as text*, which fires no trigger. The moment a slice's state goes through the queue and apply -- which uses =org-todo= -- both hooks run.

*The open question is what the predicate should ask.* "Has keyworded descendants" and "is a grouping whose time lives elsewhere" have been the same thing until now. A slice is the first case where they part, so either =--container-heading-p= learns about by-reference members, or the two callers stop sharing one predicate.


** TODO Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 18:27]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-14 Fri 13:51] \\
  confirmed surviving; three dependents
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-marker-desync-guard.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, hit live during this session's TODO.org commit-
splitting work: writing TODO.org's content directly via =git
hash-object=/=update-index= (bypassing Emacs) and relying on
=global-auto-revert-mode= to pick up the change desynced Emacs's
*in-memory* clock state from the file text. =org-clock-marker= kept
pointing at its old raw character offset, which after the rewrite
landed on an unrelated =:SESSIONS:= line instead of the =CLOCK:= line
— =org_clock_out= then failed outright with "Clock start time is
gone", and the statusline (which reads live =org-clocking-p=/
=org-clock-marker= state, not the file text — see
=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string=) kept showing "clocked
in" even after a text-level recovery via
=claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= had already closed the
=CLOCK:= line on disk.

Worked around manually this time: reset
=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-hd-marker=/=org-clock-start-time=
directly via =emacsclient=, then called
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-status-hook-out= by hand since normal
=org-clock-out= never got far enough to fire it. The existing "Stale
interval recovery" design (CLAUDE.md) only repairs the *text* side of
a stale interval (crash/shutdown case, where Emacs's own in-memory
state is simply gone after a restart) — it has no equivalent for *this*
case, where Emacs is still running but its live clock state has gone
stale relative to a file it didn't itself edit. Whether the real fix
is "never write live org files outside Emacs, full stop" (a
process/discipline fix, not code) or something more automatic (e.g.
detecting the marker/text mismatch and self-healing) is a question for
planning.

*Second, distinct incident, 2026-08-05*: on
[[id:4cda6bf7-441b-47b2-b3d5-b8c7e7a207a5][Show the TODO keyword, not just clocked in/out, in the statusline]],
after a cross-session clock resume (see the =d150c02e= sibling below)
followed by a proper =org_clock_out=, the =:LOGBOOK:= briefly
contained a genuinely malformed line — bare =CLOCK:= with *no*
timestamp at all — sitting between two valid entries, before
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= rebuilt the drawer and it
disappeared (the arithmetic of what remained checks out, so nothing
was silently lost, but *why* a timestamp-less =CLOCK:= line got
written in the first place is unconfirmed — a race between two
near-simultaneous writers to the same drawer is the working
hypothesis, not proven). A second, structurally different way the
same "shared mutable file" pressure can corrupt =:LOGBOOK:=, worth
folding into whatever planning addresses this cluster.

Also worth noting for whoever plans =4cda6bf7='s own fix: swapping
=status-label= for =org-get-todo-state= *at the clock marker* is
still marker-dependent — it inherits this exact desync mode (a stale
TODO keyword shown instead of a stale in/out label), it doesn't
route around it.

*Kept and narrowed 2026-08-13, by the user's decision.* Reviewed against
its two partial owners rather than as keep-or-cancel, because that is
what it actually is: a gap between them.

*What is retired.* The original =org_clock_out= failure is unreachable —
that function opens and closes nothing since
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]'s cutover. So is
the concurrent-writer =CLOCK:= corruption:
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= has no production caller left,
only tests.

*What survives, and is the whole of this heading now.* Two surfaces,
each only partly owned elsewhere:
1. =--trigger-auto-clock-in= (config.el:1778, registered :1941) still
   opens a real clock on *any* transition to =DOING=/=PLANNING=,
   including a human's own =C-c C-t=. Apply suppresses it via the
   re-entrancy guard; a hand-edit does not.
   [[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] covers
   *container* headings only, so leaf hand-edits remain.
2. =--statusline-task-string= (config.el:659) still reads
   =org-clocking-p=/=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-history= directly.
   [[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] is scoped to
   the statusline's *data source*, not to marker desync generally.

Cancel this and the uncovered middle belongs to nobody. The deciding
fact against cancelling is that the desync was observed *live on
2026-08-12*, after the cutover, and recorded on =ab75d6d2= — not a
theoretical residue.

*Newly established 2026-08-13, and it raises this heading's stakes.*
Reading =org-clock.el= for
[[id:803314aa-eda9-4d48-9e43-b3eb4802a4e8][803314aa]] found that
=org-clock-out= signals =(error "Clock start time is gone")=
(org-clock.el:1841) exactly when =org-clock-marker='s line is no longer
a =CLOCK:= line — i.e. precisely this heading's desync. That is the same
error incident 1 above recorded. So this desync is not merely a display
problem: it is the reachable trigger for apply's fabricated-interval
bug. The two headings are one causal chain, and this one is upstream.

*Confirmed surviving, and promoted out of =MAYBE=, 2026-08-14 by
[[id:e51d6ba1-cc1d-4745-8aa7-e4b1183a230b][e51d6ba1]]* — the heading
whose job was to decide whether this one is superseded. It is not, and
three separate things now depend on it, which is more than "someday"
can carry:

1. Observed *live on 2026-08-12*, after the cutover, so it is residue of
   nothing.
2. It is the upstream half of the causal chain to =803314aa=, per the
   paragraph directly above.
3. [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]'s decision to
   *keep* =claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= rests explicitly on
