Set live on 2026-08-11 via =M-:= to
=~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org=, which is what makes
[[id:47c027d2-7b5a-4de0-baf4-f992f3b814bd][47c027d2]]'s reworked
=org_capture= actually aim at this project. It is *not* in
=~/.config/doom/config.el=, so it lasts exactly as long as this Emacs
process and then silently reverts to =org-default-notes-file=.

One line, in the =after! org= block alongside the other org settings:

: (setq claude-code-ide-org-capture-file "~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org")

*Captured because nothing else would have surfaced it.* The only prior
mention was on =47c027d2=, which is now =DONE= — a note in a completed
heading that no future session reads. And the failure mode is quiet:
capture keeps working, it just writes somewhere else, exactly as it did
before anyone noticed. This is the inverse of the usual reload
precondition — the volatile layer is the one that looks permanent.

*Watch when it is made permanent:* this file is reachable by two paths —
=~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org= and the symlink
=~/org/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org= that puts it in =org-agenda-files=.
Capture registers new ids against the capture path while agenda scanning
finds them under the symlink path. Both resolve to the same file so
lookups should work, but it is worth confirming that =org-id-locations=
does not end up with two entries per id, and worth deciding which path
is canonical rather than letting it depend on who wrote last.

*Measured 2026-08-15, and it makes the failure concrete rather than
abstract:* =org-default-notes-file= is =/Users/neil.best/org/notes.org=.
So the "silently reverts" above is not a vague risk — on the next Emacs
restart every *target-less* =org_capture= writes into =notes.org=,
the file that exists specifically for work belonging to no project.

*Narrow exposure, worth stating so the urgency is not overestimated:*
only target-less capture is affected. Every capture made on 2026-08-15
passed an explicit =target=, and those are unaffected by the defcustom
entirely.

*But pinning it in the Doom config is a stopgap, not the fix, and the
heading above should not be read as endorsing it.* A pinned value
hardcodes *one repo's path* into a machine-global Emacs configuration.
That is the same shape as everything
[[id:9ae4b17e-278c-482f-9114-df28b2254806][9ae4b17e]] is about: the
moment a second project exists, the pin is wrong for it, and wrong in
exactly the quiet way described above — capture keeps working, it just
aims at the wrong file. Fixing the ephemerality by making the wrong
value permanent is not obviously an improvement on having it evaporate.

*The shape that would actually hold:* resolve the capture target from
context rather than from a global. The session's =cwd= is already in
every hook payload — see
[[id:83b3cdd6-982a-4ae1-878c-79a8c941f638][83b3cdd6]] — so "the org file
belonging to the project this session is running in" is derivable rather
than configured. That also removes the =org-default-notes-file= fallback
from the path entirely, which is the part that makes the failure silent.

*Recorded because the question was asked directly:* "why do you suggest
we pin the capture file in the doom config?" The honest answer is that
the suggestion was inherited from this heading and repeated without being
evaluated. It solves the ephemerality and creates a portability problem
in its place.

*The failure this heading predicted has now happened, and it is the
cause of two defects filed elsewhere as if they were separate.* Measured
2026-08-20: =claude-code-ide-org-capture-file= is =nil=,
=--capture-target-file= resolves to =/Users/neil.best/org/notes.org=,
and the Doom config still contains no mention of it. The =M-:= of
2026-08-11 died with that Emacs process, exactly as written above.

*Both symptoms follow from it:*

- A capture with no =target= wrote
  [[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]] into
  =~/org/notes.org=, outside the repository, where it sat unnoticed
  until someone went looking. That was attributed to
  [[id:97696fc2-f07b-4586-b280-de6a7d26fb87][97696fc2]], which is about
  the fallback writing a *level-1* heading — a real defect, but a
  different one, and it only bites when the capture file is =TODO.org=
  in the first place.
- A capture *with* a target failed: =target: "Clock lifecycle &
  visibility"= was refused as "neither a known =:ID:= nor a top-level
  heading in *notes.org*". The title form resolves against the capture
  file, so while that file is =notes.org= no category of =TODO.org= can
  be named at all.

*So the "org_capture cannot target a category" problem is this heading,
not a design limitation.* With the capture file pointing at =TODO.org=,
the categories *are* its top-level headings and the title form works.
The one-line fix above removes both symptoms.

*What it cost in the meantime*, worth recording because the workaround
looked reasonable each time: every heading created this session was
written by hand-inserted elisp instead, which is what left =org-id=
stale in [[id:8ddd7fa8-e847-42e9-b82c-63bbe4c11b7b][8ddd7fa8]] and what
produced the inline-keyword habit behind
[[id:cc0c17a7-a554-426d-afeb-09cbee7339f0][cc0c17a7]]. An unpersisted
=defcustom= propagated into three other headings before anyone asked
what it was actually part of.

*Persisted 2026-08-20.* One =setq= in the =after! org= block of
=~/.config/doom/config.el=, and set live in the running Emacs so the
tool works now.

*The path question is answered, and not the way the line above proposes.*
That line names =~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org=. Measured instead:
=claude-code-ide-org--tracked-files= returns
=/Users/neil.best/org/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org=, =org-agenda-files=
holds the same, and only =file-truename= gives the =~/git/= path. Every
other part of this system therefore knows the file by the *symlink*
name, because =org-agenda-files= is built by scanning =org-directory=.
Pointing capture at the git path is precisely what would give =org-id=
two entries per id — the divergence this heading warned about, not one
to accept. Written as
=(expand-file-name "claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org" org-directory)= so it
tracks =org-directory= rather than restating it.

*The "narrow exposure" paragraph above is wrong and is corrected here.*
It says only *target-less* capture is affected. Measured 2026-08-19: a
capture carrying an explicit =target= of ="Clock lifecycle &
visibility"= was refused as "neither a known =:ID:= nor a top-level
heading in *notes.org*". A title target resolves against the capture
file, so while that file was =notes.org= no category could be named at
all. Only *:ID:* targets were unaffected — which is why the captures of
2026-08-15 all worked, and why the claim looked true.

:END:
*Verified by using it, not by reading it:*
[[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]] was filed with
=target: "Skill logic"= and landed as a level-2 heading under that
category. The same call shape failed the day before.

*One claim still open, deliberately.* Persistence itself is unverified:
setting the value live proves the tool, not the config. The =after! org=
block only runs at startup, and per the org-dev skill a check against a
layer that has not reloaded is indistinguishable from a passing one. The
next session verifies it for free — if capture works without anyone
setting anything, it persisted.

*Verified 2026-08-20, and the first check was not a verification.* After the user ran =doom/reload=, reading the variable showed the configured value — but a reload cannot disprove the alternative, because the live =setq= from the session that filed this heading would have survived it just as well. The discriminating test is to unset first: =(setq claude-code-ide-org-capture-file nil)=, then =(load (expand-file-name "config.el" doom-user-dir))=, then read. Result =(:before nil :after "/Users/neil.best/org/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org")=. The config sets it.

*The symlink-versus-real-path argument recorded earlier does not hold, and nothing depends on it.* The value was set to the =~/org/= symlink path on the reasoning that =--tracked-files= and =org-agenda-files= use it. But =find-file-visit-truename= is =t= here, so Emacs resolves the symlink on open: the buffer visiting either path is one buffer, named for the truename =~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org=. Either value would have worked. Keeping the symlink form for consistency with the surrounding settings, not because it is load-bearing.

*Discovered by the same check, and separately useful:* =get-file-buffer= on the symlink path returns nil while a buffer for that file is plainly open, because it matches on =buffer-file-name= rather than the truename. Any future code that asks \"is this file already open?\" by that route will be wrong here.


** TODO The org 9.7+ dependency is real, undeclared, and unenforced :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       1ed7b2b4-a809-4547-b04f-099a3111fc1e
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 13:35]
:END:

*Raised by the user 2026-08-14,* on reading
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]]'s probe results
and asking whether the org version it pins is a dependency, and whether
anything says so. Measured immediately, and the answer is that it is
real and nothing says so anywhere.

*The dependency, confirmed live rather than recalled.* Against the
bundled org that =emacs --batch -Q= loads on this machine:

: org-version: 9.6.15
:   org-element-type-p           fboundp=nil
:   org-element-contents-end     fboundp=nil
:   org-element-at-point         fboundp=t

Against the straight-managed org Doom and =bin/test= use: =9.8.7=, with
both bound. =config.el= calls =org-element-type-p= three times and
=org-element-contents-end= twice — all five in the drawer code — so on
9.6.15 the module does not degrade, it hits =void-function=. Both
accessors arrived in org 9.7, which is the actual floor.

*Where it is expressed today: nowhere that binds.* =packages.el= names
only =org-ql= and =claude-code-ide=, and never mentions org. There is no
=version<= check, no =org-assert-version=, no minimum in any header.
The only two places that know are indirect — =bin/test='s glob for the
straight =build-*= directory, and a troubleshooting paragraph in the
org-dev skill explaining why a test might behave as though a newer org
feature does not exist.

*Why that is worse than it sounds.* Both indirect mechanisms fail
*silently*. The org-dev skill already records that =bin/test='s glob
"silently falls back to the bundled org again with no error", and the
symptom is tests behaving as if a newer org feature is missing — which
is indistinguishable from the feature being broken. The glob does
resolve today (=build-29.4=), so nothing is wrong right now; the point
is that nothing would say so if it stopped.

*Not yet decided, and the options differ in kind:* an =org-assert-version=
or explicit =version<= guard at load time (fails loudly, on the right
machine, at the right moment); a declared minimum in =packages.el=
(documents intent, enforces nothing under straight); or making
=bin/test= refuse to run rather than fall back when its glob misses
(catches it in CI-shaped usage but never at a user's install). They are
not exclusive, and the first is the only one that protects someone
installing this module on an Emacs whose org nobody here controls —
which is the same argument
[[id:4f9e552a-e1a9-48dd-8801-3f23a31b9e32][4f9e552a]] made for keeping
the =command emacs= convention after the environment fix moved out.

*Related:* [[id:1caed585-3209-4108-8501-eba16baf6a01][1caed585]] asks
what this module may change on a user's machine when installed; this is
the narrower question of what it may *assume*. And
[[id:d7849119-8224-4993-a8ab-83e1434df53c][d7849119]]'s README would be
the natural place a stated minimum lands for a human reader.


** TODO Concurrent sessions share one git index, and explicit staging protects nothing :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       2aeb65d6-350e-4c75-811e-4a748044fe2c
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 16:20]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from              [2026-08-12 Wed 16:22] \\
  user is blocked on this: no second session until a mechanism exists
- State "TODO"       from "NEXT"       [2026-08-17 Mon 12:11] \\
  Auto-demoted: superseded by sibling [[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][Lint for headings that are valid org but structurally unintended]] becoming NEXT.
:END:

*This one actually happened, deliberately, as a resilience test.* On
2026-08-12 two sessions ran against this same checkout. This session
wrote a heading to TODO.org, staged it with an explicit pathspec
(=git add -- TODO.org=), and went to commit. The commit failed with
=nothing to commit, working tree clean=: the other session had already
committed, and its pathspec-less =git commit= took the whole index,
sweeping this session's 69 staged lines into
=a8dc693 "Review-buffer interaction fixes ..."= — a commit whose
message describes unrelated work and names two unrelated heading
=:ID:=s. Content survived; attribution did not. =36410e2= is an empty
commit recording that, since =a8dc693= was already pushed and
rewriting a pushed commit under a live concurrent session is worse
than the mislabelling.

*The mechanism is the index, not the files.* Every convention this
project already has is about file contents — check for a modified
buffer, stage explicitly, never =git add -A=. None of them touch the
actual shared mutable state, which is =.git/index=: one per checkout,
not one per session. Worse, following the convention *increased* the
damage. Staging early loaded this session's work into the index
*before* the other session committed; a pathspec-less =git commit= then
picked it up automatically. Had this session not staged at all, its
work would have been left alone.

*Not covered by the existing worktree heading, and this is the load-
bearing part.*
[[id:2e09adb7-6e29-499c-a40e-98cd2b327cff][2e09adb7]] concludes that
worktrees handle the *code* side — "one checkout + branch per session,
no shared-file collisions" — and that only the org files need to stay a
single canonical copy in the main checkout. Read against this incident
those two halves collide: the file that got swept up *was* TODO.org,
which by that heading's own =:ID:=-uniqueness and clock-fragmentation
argument is exactly the file that *cannot* be partitioned into a
worktree. So a worktree session must still write and commit TODO.org
in the main checkout, through the main checkout's index, and hits this
identically. *Worktrees do not fix this scenario; they fix everything
around it.* That is new information for 2e09adb7, which assumed the
code side was the whole problem.

*Related but distinct:*
[[id:3cb3f955-f10a-47cd-84ab-e629d73ea59d][3cb3f955]] already asks
whether =git commit -- TODO.org= is "safe," but scoped to a *single*
session not sweeping up its own unrelated hunks in the same file. This
heading is the cross-session case: another session's *staged* work, in
any file. Same command, different failure. Worth answering together.

*One mitigation is already verified.* Tested 2026-08-12 in a scratch
repo: with =A.txt= staged, =git commit -- B.txt= commits B only, leaves
A staged, and does not commit A. So a pathspec-scoped commit is
genuinely safe for the *other* session's work. The catch is that it
constrains the wrong party — safety depends on the *committing* session
complying, and the session at risk has no way to enforce it.

*Caveat found while defining the hook below:* =git commit -- <paths>=
*fails on an untracked file* — =error: pathspec 'NEW.txt' did not match
any file(s) known to git=. A new file must be =git add=-ed first, and
only then can be committed by pathspec. So for brand-new content (a new
plan, a new script) the staging window this mitigation exists to avoid
is unavoidable, and exposure persists between the add and the commit.
The mitigation is complete only for already-tracked files — which is
where this incident happened, TODO.org being tracked.

*Options for something robust, none chosen:*
- *Convention only:* ban =git add= outright in favour of
  =git commit -- <paths>=, in CLAUDE.md and the org skill. Cheapest,
  verified to work, and binds only sessions that follow it. Given both
  sessions here are Claude Code reading the same CLAUDE.md, this is
  less hollow than it sounds — but it is guidance, not a mechanism, and
  the user asked for a mechanism.
- *A =pre-commit= hook* in =.githooks/=. Defined below — the only
  option here that is a mechanism rather than guidance, and cheaper
  than first assumed.
- *Route git writes through the event queue* of
  [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]. That queue
  already carries =session_id=/=agent_id= and already exists to stop
  concurrent sessions from mutating shared state directly, with a human
  applying batches later. Commits are the same shape of problem. The
  heaviest option and the most consistent with where the project is
  already going.

*The =pre-commit= option, defined.* Measured 2026-08-12 in a scratch
repo with a hook that printed its own environment. =GIT_INDEX_FILE=
identifies which command form invoked the commit:

| Command                  | =GIT_INDEX_FILE=            | Sweeps a staged file? |
|--------------------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------|
| =git commit=             | =.git/index=                | yes                   |
| =git commit --amend=     | =.git/index=                | yes                   |
| =git commit -a=          | =.git/index.lock=           | yes                   |
| =git commit -- <paths>=  | =.git/next-index-<pid>.lock= | no                   |

The hook can read *both* views at once: what the commit will contain
(=git diff --cached=) and what the real index holds
(=GIT_INDEX_FILE=$(git rev-parse --git-dir)/index git diff --cached=).
In the pathspec case those differ exactly as they should — commit
=B.txt=, real index still =A.txt=. =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID= also
propagates into the hook, confirmed by printing it.

*Proposed rule:* refuse when the form is a whole-index one (basename of
=GIT_INDEX_FILE= is =index= or =index.lock=) *and* the real index is
non-empty; fire only when =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID= is set. That is the
sweep case and nothing else. =git commit -- <paths>= always passes;
=git commit -a= against an empty index passes, since no one's work is at
risk; a human committing from a plain terminal is never affected. The
message should name the staged paths and print the pathspec form to use
instead.

*This retires the "needs ownership tracking" objection*, which the
first version of this heading asserted and which is wrong: the command
*form* is a sufficient discriminator and the safe form is
self-identifying. Per-session ownership of staged paths would only be
needed for a more permissive rule ("allow the sweep if everything
staged is mine"), not worth building first. If it ever is, the
precedent for where such state lives is
=claude-code-ide-org-queue-directory= — per-session runtime files,
deliberately outside the repo.

*Known limitations, all of them accepted rather than solved:*
- =--amend= is *indistinguishable* from a plain pathspec-less commit —
  both report =.git/index= — so it gets refused too, absent an escape
  hatch such as an =ALLOW_SWEEP= environment variable.
- =--no-verify= bypasses the hook entirely. This makes the mistake loud
  rather than silent; it does not make it impossible, and it still
  constrains the committing party rather than protecting the session at
  risk.
- Merge/rebase/cherry-pick paths are *untested* — the scratch-repo run
  errored before reaching them, and they need checking before shipping.
- Opt-in per clone via =core.hooksPath=, inheriting the same setup
  caveat =pre-push= already has.

*Testing* would follow the existing =bin/*-test= pattern
(=sync-plans-test=, =queue-append-test=): a scratch repo, each of the
four command forms, asserting exit status and message.

*Blocked on this in practice:* the user has stated they will not run a
second concurrent session until a mechanism is in place, so this gates
concurrent work generally rather than being one more backlog item.

- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 17:48] \\
  backdated: container entered DOING when its first child 8d682a5b did
:END:
:PLAN:

One arc in three steps: find the structural drift that already exists,
teach the linter to catch that class, then stop new instances reaching
history.

The three were filed separately over two weeks and only read as a set
once =7e9aa4ea= joined them on 2026-08-19. Composed rather than left
scattered because they must land in this order — the diagnosis decides
what rules are worth writing, and enforcing rules nobody has validated
would block commits on false positives.

*Evidence from one session, 2026-08-18/19.* Five structural errors were
introduced and committed: four =:ID:= UUIDs written from memory, each
with a correct 8-character prefix and a wrong tail, and one line of
prose that wrapped so =30.= began a line and org parsed it as an
ordered-list bullet. All five were caught by =bin/lint-org= *after* the
commit, each needing a follow-up. That is the case for the third step
and it is also a rough measure of the rate.

Every member already carries its own detail; this heading deliberately
holds only what the set shares.

:END:
*All three children done, 2026-08-19.* The arc held: the diagnosis
decided what rules were worth writing, and the rules were validated
before being enforced.

- [[id:8d682a5b-1b03-4744-995c-a871a8b40ddc][8d682a5b]] — all eleven
  org-lint reports were markdown bold, none a misplaced heading. Fixing
  them took 28 warnings to 18, and had to convert twenty-one spans
  rather than the ten flagged, because org-lint only reports a closing
  marker followed by a space and the ten are entangled with the rest.
- [[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][95087d8f]] — two checks org
  had no opinion on. The second needed neither design this heading
  proposed: categories identify themselves by carrying =:ARCHIVE:=.
- [[id:7e9aa4ea-4757-4e58-bac3-3c3e67fc4cb1][7e9aa4ea]] — the commit
  gate, scoped to staged content, verified by refusing a real commit.

*Ordering was load-bearing, as expected.* Had the gate landed first it
would have been enforcing rules nobody had validated, against a corpus
carrying eighteen unexamined warnings.

*Backdated into DOING* at 17:48, when 8d682a5b started, rather than at
composition — see
[[id:241a4c54-ed59-4780-b602-2a86efee978a][241a4c54]] for why that rule
is written nowhere and why writing it down is not trivial. No clock: a
container's own CLOCK line lands inside the total org already rolls up
from its children.

- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-19 Wed 18:36] \\
  all 11 diagnosed and the class fixed
:END:
:PLAN:

*Surfaced 2026-08-14 the moment =org-lint= was wired into
[[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]'s script,* which
is the whole argument for having run it sooner.

=org-lint= reports "Possibly misplaced heading line" at =TODO.org= lines
3304, 3323, 4067, 4074, 4077, 4097, 4183, 6875, 7709 and 9785, and
=DONE.org= 743. None is diagnosed yet. (A twelfth report, a bullet-
counter complaint at =TODO.org= 822, is unrelated and also unexamined.)

*Why it is worth diagnosing rather than silencing.* One instance of this
exact shape was found by hand the same day: a heading glued to the end
of a prose line (=f39944f=, 2026-08-13), which org does not parse as a
heading at all — so it is invisible to =org-agenda=, =org_outline= and
every =org_*= tool, its =:ID:= is unreachable, and links to it dangle,
while the raw text still *looks* correct to a reader. If even one of
these eleven is that, a tracked heading is missing from the system
without anyone knowing.

*Expect false positives too.* The checker hedges in its own wording, and
these files quote org syntax in prose constantly — example blocks,
inline bold at line start, the transition tables. Sorting real from
quoted is the actual work, and the answer decides whether these become
errors or stay warnings.

:END:
*Diagnosed 2026-08-19. All eleven are markdown-style =**bold**= in files
whose bold is =*single*=. None is a misplaced heading.*

=org-lint-misplaced-heading= searches for =[^*\n\r ,]\*\*+ = — an
ordinary character, two or more asterisks, then a space — as a heuristic
for the corruption its own docstring illustrates:

: ** Heading 1** Oops heading 2

What it actually catches here is the *closing* =**= of a markdown bold
span followed by a space. Running that regex over both files returns
exactly eleven lines, matching the eleven reports one for one:

| file     |                                            lines |
|----------+--------------------------------------------------|
| TODO.org |                                       2505, 6304 |
| DONE.org | 3047, 3058, 3649, 3656, 3660, 3669, 3757, 6079, 9363 |

*Ten are real defects; one is not.* =DONE.org:9363= names a level-4 heading inside verbatim markup —
a true false positive. The other ten are markdown habit leaking into
org.

*And they are visible defects, not merely stylistic.* Checked in this
Emacs: org parses =**bold**= as a =bold= element whose *contents* are
the literal string =*bold*=. So it renders as bold text with stray
asterisks around it, which is not what any of the ten authors intended.

*The finding for [[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][95087d8f]]*:
org-lint already detects this class — the problem is that it reports it
under a name describing a different and much more alarming defect.
Anyone reading "Possibly misplaced heading line" goes looking for broken
structure and finds prose. A local checker naming it "markdown bold in
an org file" would cost one regex and would not need org-lint's
heuristic at all.

*Not fixed here.* Ten mechanical edits collapsing a doubled asterisk
to a single one, nine of them in
=DONE.org=, would take the warning count from 28 to 18. Left as its own
decision because the spans cross line breaks in at least one case
\(TODO.org 2504-2505\) and because editing archived prose deserves to be
asked for rather than assumed.

*Fixed 2026-08-19* (=4f25341=). Warnings 28 -> 18. Twenty-one spans
converted, not ten — and that was forced rather than chosen.

*The flagged subset cannot be fixed on its own.* org-lint only reports a
closing marker followed by a space, so the ten it names are scattered
through a class of twenty-one whose members share delimiters. A first
attempt to fix only the ten matched from one span's opening across to a
later span's closing and collapsed the outer pair, leaving
=DONE.org:3051= and =3058= each with one marker of the wrong width. That
was reverted.

*What worked:* pair the markers properly. Walk every doubled asterisk
that is not inside verbatim markup and not part of a longer run;
classify as opening when the next character is not whitespace and
closing when the previous one is not; pair within a paragraph. Twenty-one
spans, each read before applying.

Left alone, correctly: glob patterns inside =paths:= values, the
verbatim mention that names the marker itself, the level-4 heading in
=DONE.org:9363=, and eight example heading lines inside fixed-width
blocks. =DONE.org= now holds no doubled asterisk outside verbatim at
all; the eight left in =TODO.org= are all examples.

*Verification worth reusing:* every changed line, with asterisks
stripped, matches its predecessor exactly — so the edit provably changed
nothing but emphasis markers. That check is what caught the corruption
the first time, and it is cheap.

CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 18:47]--[2026-08-19 Wed 18:55] =>  0:08
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-19 Wed 18:47] \\
  add the two structural lint checks
- [2026-08-19 Wed 18:47]--[2026-08-19 Wed 18:55] punctuation-only titles and stray level-1 headings
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-19 Wed 19:23] \\
  re-queued; prior event went stale on from
:END:
:PLAN:

Two checks =bin/lint-org= does not have, both identified 2026-08-17 after
a single line of prose re-parented 6462 lines of TODO.org and survived
two days, a full review-and-apply, and repeated clean lint runs.

*The incident, stated once so the checks have something concrete to be
tested against.* =* * *= was written into
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]'s closing summary
as a horizontal rule -- the markdown idiom, reached for without thinking.
In org a =*= at column 0 is a headline, so it became a *level-1 category
titled "* *"* which swallowed lines 3174--9636: sixteen level-2 headings
and the queue epic's thirty-four children, none of which belonged to it.

*=lint-org= reported =0 errors= throughout, correctly.* The file was
valid org the entire time. That is the whole point of these two checks:
the existing suite asks whether the file parses and whether this
project's conventions hold on the headings it finds, and neither question
notices a heading that should not exist at all.

*Check 1: a heading title that is punctuation only.* =* *= has no word
characters. No real heading in either file does, and none plausibly
would. Cheap, and it catches the exact shape that occurred -- at any
level, not just level 1.

*Check 2: a level-1 heading outside the file's known categories.*
Stronger and more general: level-1 headings in TODO.org are categories,
a small and slow-changing set, and one appearing mid-file is almost
certainly an accident. Would have caught this even if the phantom had
been named something word-like. Needs a decision about how the known set
is expressed -- enumerated in the check, or inferred as "every level-1
that existed at the last clean run", the latter being self-maintaining
but only as trustworthy as the run that seeded it.

*Where they go.* =claude-code-ide-org--lint-file= (config.el:5501) is
where the per-file conventions are checked; =bin/lint-org= is the 84-line
shell wrapper and needs no change. Per that script's own header, each
check wants a fixture that violates it -- "a lint whose assertions have
never been seen to fail proves nothing".

*Filed as one heading covering both* because they are one change to one
check suite, tested the same way, arising from one incident. Split them
if either turns out to want its own design decision -- check 2 plausibly
does, over how the known category set is defined.

*Related but distinct:*
[[id:8d682a5b-1b03-4744-995c-a871a8b40ddc][8d682a5b]] is about
diagnosing org-lint's *existing* misplaced-heading reports; this is about
adding checks org-lint has no opinion on.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-19* (=cc25adc=). Both checks live in
=claude-code-ide-org--lint-file=; =bin/lint-org= needed no change, as
predicted. Replayed against the incident by injecting =* *= into a copy
of the real =TODO.org=: each check flags it independently.

*Check 2 needed neither option this heading offered.* The choice recorded
here was an enumerated category list or a set inferred from the last
clean run. The categories already identify themselves: measured on the
real files, all seven level-1 headings in =TODO.org= carry =:ARCHIVE:=
and none of =DONE.org='s seven do, because the latter are archive
targets. So the routing set is collected across every linted file and
matched by title — a target is recognised by the source it mirrors. No
enumeration to maintain, no seed state whose trustworthiness depends on
the run that made it, and adding a category keeps the check correct for
free.

*One constraint this heading did not anticipate.* The suite's own
fixtures use a bare =* Category= with no =:ARCHIVE:=, so an
unconditional rule fails its positive control. The check is skipped when
no routing level-1 exists anywhere in the linted set: with nothing to
infer the convention from, saying nothing is the honest answer. It costs
nothing here, where seven categories route.

*Both got a violating fixture*, per this file's rule. Four mutations
checked; three discriminated at once. The fourth — removing the mirror
allowance — failed nothing, because its fixture's only level-1 carried
=:ARCHIVE:= so the allowance was never reached. Replaced with a two-file
fixture, the only shape in which a mirror exists, and it discriminates.

*And the trap documented three lines above the new function was walked
into anyway:* =org-map-entries= resolves =file= scope through
=buffer-file-name=, which a temp buffer has not got, so it maps over
nothing and reports that as success. The collector silently returned no
categories and the check silently passed. Scope nil, with the comment
repeated at the new call site.

=bin/test= 321/321; =bin/lint-org= unchanged at 0 errors / 18 warnings,
so neither check false-positives on the real corpus.

*Not split.* Both landed as one change, as this heading allowed for —
check 2's design decision resolved itself once the =:ARCHIVE:= property
turned out to be the discriminator.


*** DONE Run bin/lint-org in a pre-commit hook, scoped to staged org files :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-19 Wed 19:30]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7e9aa4ea-4757-4e58-bac3-3c3e67fc4cb1
:CREATED:  [2026-08-19 Wed 10:56]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 10:54]--[2026-08-19 Wed 11:05] =>  0:11
- [2026-08-19 Wed 10:54]--[2026-08-19 Wed 11:05] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 11:14]--[2026-08-19 Wed 11:16] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-19 Wed 11:14]--[2026-08-19 Wed 11:16] assigned at review
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 18:56] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 19:27]--[2026-08-19 Wed 19:30] =>  0:03
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-19 Wed 19:27] \\
  write the pre-commit lint hook
- [2026-08-19 Wed 19:27]--[2026-08-19 Wed 19:30] pre-commit hook scoped to staged org files
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-19 Wed 19:30] \\
  hook shipped, tested, verified live
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:14]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:21] =>  0:07
- [2026-08-20 Thu 16:14]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:21] assigned at review
:END:
:PLAN:

=bin/lint-org= runs nowhere automatically — not in a hook, not in
=bin/test=. The only git hook is =.githooks/pre-push=, which checks the
plans archive. =core.hooksPath= is already set to =.githooks=, so a new
hook needs no setup step.

*Evidence it would pay.* Five org errors were introduced and committed
across 2026-08-18/19: four =:ID:= UUIDs written from memory (correct
8-character prefix, wrong tail, every time) and one line of prose that
wrapped so =30.= began a line, which org parsed as an ordered-list
bullet. All five were caught by =bin/lint-org= *after* the commit and
each needed a follow-up commit. Runtime is 1.6s over both files.

*Scope to staged =.org= files, linting their staged content.* The
=pre-push= hook's own header argues against pre-commit for =sync-plans=
on two grounds. Neither survives here: it would mutate the index —
linting mutates nothing; and it would couple unrelated commits — true
only of a whole-tree lint. Scoped to staged paths, an elisp-only commit
lints nothing and costs nothing, and a concurrent session's unstaged org
edit cannot block it. Linting the staged content rather than the
worktree is also the more correct semantics: the check should describe
what is about to be committed.

*Fail on errors only.* 28 warnings are currently accepted and would
block every commit.

*Why not pre-push instead*, which is where this project puts its other
check: pre-push is too late for the observed harm. All five errors were
caught before any push; the cost was noise in local history, which only
a pre-commit hook prevents. Whether pre-push should lint as well, for
anything that reached history via =--no-verify=, is open and cheap.

*Decided 2026-08-19: block the human too.* The caveat about blocking the
user on an error they did not write was raised and dismissed by them —
=git commit --no-verify= is the escape hatch, and
=.githooks/pre-push= already establishes the convention, documenting the
bypass in a header comment and printing =Skip: git push --no-verify= in
its own failure message. The new hook should print its equivalent.

Two things that decision does not cover, both worth building in:

- *The hook is not author-aware*, and cannot be. It lints staged =.org=
  content, so it blocks whoever runs =git commit= regardless of who
  introduced the error. That is the right behaviour — the committer is
  the one publishing it — but it means "block me on my errors" is really
  "block the commit on the file's errors".
- *Claude must never pass =--no-verify=.* All five errors this hook is
  being built for were Claude's, so a bypass Claude may use defeats the
  hook entirely. The escape hatch belongs to the human alone, and that
  needs to be a written rule rather than an assumption, because it costs
  nothing to type and would silently restore the status quo.

*Gotcha to document in the hook itself:* it lints the *staged* content,
not the Emacs buffer. =TODO.org= is normally open and sometimes has
unsaved changes — that is why =org_amend= has a deferred path at all —
so a clean lint at commit time says nothing about what is still only in
the buffer.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-19* (=4b22f72=), as =.githooks/pre-commit= with
=bin/pre-commit-test= beside it. =core.hooksPath= was already set, so it
took effect with no setup step.

*Verified live in this repo, not only in a fixture:* appending =* *= to
=TODO.org= and committing was refused, reporting both checks added that
afternoon under [[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][95087d8f]],
and nothing landed. The commit that added the hook itself stages no org,
so it exited immediately — the free path, demonstrated by the change
that introduced it.

Seven tests, of which two pin the staged-versus-worktree distinction in
both directions: a clean staged file with a broken worktree passes, and a
broken staged file with a clean worktree refuses. That is the case a
worktree-based check would get backwards.

*The first draft failed every passing case.* It used =mapfile=, an
associative array, and a bare =\"${arr[@]}\"= on an empty array — all
bash 4 or later. macOS ships 3.2 and =#!/usr/bin/env bash= resolves to
it, so the refusing cases passed for the wrong reason while every
should-pass case exited 1. Rewritten for 3.2, with the constraint stated
in the hook's own header.
[[id:84b7d8b3-5161-4146-9167-0e7e2100e365][84b7d8b3]] is the standing
shell question; this is it arriving in practice, and it is evidence that
project scripts are not currently written against a stated baseline.


** TODO A duration assertion matches the clocktable's own timestamp, three minutes a day :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       5a5e87c9-f432-44c6-a5a6-f6f3755e4347
:CREATED:  [2026-08-20 Thu 11:02]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 10:50]--[2026-08-20 Thu 11:09] =>  0:19
- [2026-08-20 Thu 10:50]--[2026-08-20 Thu 11:09] assigned at review
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-20 Thu 16:44] \\
  captured keywordless; state never set
:END:

=claude-code-ide-org-clock-report= prefixes its table with =#+CAPTION: Clock summary at [2026-08-20 Thu 11:00], ...=. Two tests assert on durations with =(string-match-p "1:00" result)= against that whole string, so the caption's own clock satisfies the match.

Observed, not theorised: =test-clock-report-block-today= and =test-clock-report-explicit-tstart-tend= passed at 10:59:52 on 2026-08-20, failed at 11:00:12, 11:00:20 and 11:00:44, then passed again at 11:01. The failing assertion is the negative one, =(should (not (string-match-p "1:00" result)))=, which is the shape a green suite cannot catch: it only fires when the substring appears for the wrong reason.

Three minutes a day for this duration -- 01:00, 11:00, 21:00. Other durations asserted the same way have the same exposure at their own minutes.

Approach: assert against the table row, =| *Total time* | *0:00* |=, rather than the whole report. Stripping the caption would also work but matching the row is what the assertion means.

/Body restored 2026-08-20 by =org_amend=; the original capture silently discarded it -- see/ [[id:1cd2df62-35e9-47dc-bcce-dda7bae3c522][1cd2df62]].


** DONE Every orgit link a body adds costs a permanent lint warning :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-25 Tue 11:46]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       43201e64-3fe8-4fa6-81f5-d2ab5b65d191
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 16:07]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 17:11]--[2026-08-21 Fri 17:17] =>  0:06
- [2026-08-21 Fri 17:11]--[2026-08-21 Fri 17:17] assigned at review
- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-25 Tue 11:46] \\
  stub link types registered in the lint
:END:

The commit-reference convention shipped 2026-08-21 ([[id:7b54c439-ab2c-4398-b332-2b2cc0d98ac9][7b54c439]]) says to link commits in org bodies as =orgit-rev:=. Its *first actual use* -- one link on [[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]] the same evening -- pushed =bin/lint-org= from 23 warnings to 24: =org-lint: Unknown fuzzy location "orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::f0cdc90"=. The two earlier occurrences in TODO.org are inside =verbatim= markup and are never parsed as links, so nothing had exercised the convention until now.

*Cause.* =orgit= is a Doom package present in the live Emacs, but =bin/lint-org= runs =emacs --batch -Q= with only org and =config.el= loaded. With no =orgit-rev= link type registered, org-lint falls back to treating it as a fuzzy internal link and cannot resolve it.

*Why it matters more than one warning.* The cost is per-link and permanent, so following a documented convention makes the report grow monotonically -- and a report that accumulates stops being read, which is the exact failure [[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] measured and the lint's own error/warn split exists to prevent. Candidate fixes, none chosen: load orgit in the lint's =emacs_args=, register the link type with a stub =org-link-set-parameters=, or filter this org-lint check. The first is honest but adds a package dependency to a batch script that deliberately has none.

*Fixed 2026-08-25* by the second of the three candidates recorded above -- registering the link types with a stub =org-link-set-parameters= in =bin/lint-org=, rather than loading orgit or filtering the check. 29 warnings to 28, and zero =orgit= matches in the report.

*The first candidate was rejected on the reason this heading already gave.* =(require 'orgit)= would drag magit into a batch script that deliberately carries no package dependencies, to buy nothing the lint uses: org-lint does not need the link to *work*, only to know the scheme exists so it stops guessing the location is fuzzy. The stub is three lines and registers =orgit-rev=, =orgit-log= and =orgit-status= together, so the two link types nothing has used yet are covered before their first use rather than after.

*Guarded with =unless (assoc type org-link-parameters)=*, so the stub can never clobber a real registration if the lint's environment ever does load orgit.

*Forced by [[id:c44c2119-dbf8-4510-99e2-6aaa7f259d94][c44c2119]] rather than chosen*, which is worth recording because this heading predicted it. It was filed as "small; worth doing before the convention is used much more", and sat unworked for four days. The slice's revision-link design adds four orgit links at once and one per slice thereafter, so the monotonic growth this heading warned about arrived as a concrete number the same afternoon the design was proposed.

  filed from audit garden-weeding recommendation
:END:

*Filed 2026-08-21, not built.* The 2026-08-21 cross-artifact audit found that prose conventions recur (UUID-from-memory, clock-in-at-drift) while mechanically checked ones fail loudly and get fixed -- and that duplicated conventions drift into contradiction (four found in CLAUDE.md, one in a memory, one in the next-session prompt). =bin/check-org-dev-skill= already embodies the fix for one file; generalize it: the CLAUDE.md and org-skill transition tables agree; every cited =:ID:=, defcustom, and function name resolves (=git grep= the symbol); every "is live"/"not built" claim carries a greppable anchor (e.g. =config.el:2792=) that still matches. Wire into =bin/hooks/pre-commit= beside the existing lint. Not a prose rule about keeping prose fresh -- that is the anti-pattern recursing.


** TODO A clock-report test fails every evening, because its fixture spills past midnight :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c31b6c76-8ad8-45d4-a87b-39e085e31368
:CREATED:  [2026-08-22 Sat 23:16]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-22 Sat 23:16] \\
  captured keywordless, setting initial state
:END:

*Measured 2026-08-22 at 23:16.* =claude-code-ide-org-test-clock-report-block-today= builds its fixture as "now, seconds zeroed" plus one hour, then asserts the report contains =1:00=. Run after 23:00 the interval crosses midnight, so =:block "today"= sees only the part before it and the table reads =*0:44*=:

: (string-match-p "1:00" "#+CAPTION: Clock summary at [2026-08-22 Sat 23:16], ...
: | *Total time* | *0:44* |")  => nil

So the test is red for the last hour of every day, and green the other 23. Confirmed pre-existing: it fails identically on an unmodified tree, which is how it was ruled out while [[id:eaeeb4ee-ceee-4094-8598-bbcdc8214b66][eaeeb4ee]] was being verified.

*Distinct from* [[id:5a5e87c9-f432-44c6-a5a6-f6f3755e4347][5a5e87c9]], which names this same test and is easy to mistake for it. That one is the =#+CAPTION:='s own timestamp satisfying a duration match for three minutes a day; this one is the fixture's *interval* leaving the queried window, and the assertion that fails is the positive one rather than the negative. Both are the same underlying habit -- a test whose fixture is pinned to =(current-time)= and whose assertion is a substring match over the whole report -- so they are worth fixing together even though neither is the other.

Approach: anchor the fixture to a fixed date rather than to now, which removes the dependence on when the suite runs instead of narrowing the window in which it breaks.


** REVIEW [0/1] Footnote discipline decays on re-mention; enforce it at Stop :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       2758f3a0-3c47-405e-9662-a7669dcb60cc
:CREATED:  [2026-08-24 Mon 11:28]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from              [2026-08-24 Mon 11:29] \\
  captured keywordless, nominating as next
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:38] =>  0:07
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] \\
  build the Stop-hook footnote check
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:38] build the footnote Stop hook
- State "REVIEW"     from "DOING"      [2026-08-24 Mon 12:38] \\
  shipped; cannot verify until a new session
:END:

*Measured 2026-08-24 over this session's own transcript*, after the user observed the footnotes had gone spotty. 132 assistant messages, 32 of which cited an org =:ID:=.

*Coverage: 37 of 85 citations footnoted -- 43%.*

Two findings, and the second is the actionable one.

*It is all-or-nothing per message, not sloppiness within one.* 25 of the 32 messages either footnoted every id they cited or none of them; only 7 were partial. So nothing is being forgotten mid-paragraph -- a per-message decision is flipping.

*It decays on re-mention, and every offender was correct the first time.*

| id | missed | of | first footnoted |
|----------+--------+----+-----------------|
| cbe282ec |      9 | 13 | yes             |
| 8bcd56f4 |      5 |  9 | yes             |
| 3063c3e5 |      4 |  7 | yes             |
| cc0c17a7 |      3 |  4 | yes             |
| 7771fc63 |      3 |  4 | yes             |
| aa1ba915 |      3 |  4 | yes             |

*The mechanism, stated plainly:* once an id has been footnoted, it stops reading as opaque *to the writer*, who has just looked the title up and is holding it. But the rule is per *response*, and the reader of message 122 does not have message 44 on screen. The rule's own wording -- "one footnote per distinct identifier, ordered by first appearance" -- is silent on whether "distinct" means within the response or within the conversation, and the failure lives exactly in that silence.

*Three fixes, and they are complementary rather than alternatives.*

1. *Say the quiet part in the rule*: distinct **within this response**, regardless of whether it was footnoted earlier in the conversation. One clause, aimed precisely at the measured failure. Cheapest, and it is what the data says is missing -- but memory alone is what just produced 43%.
2. *Enforce it at =Stop=.* Verified against the Claude Code hook docs rather than assumed: a =Stop= hook can prevent the turn ending, either with exit 2 or with =continue: false= plus =hookSpecificOutput.stopReason=, and the reason is shown to the model. Crucially it receives *=last_assistant_message=* as a Stop-specific input field, and the docs explicitly direct hooks needing the current turn's final text to use it rather than =transcript_path=, which lags. So the check is: scan for =\b[0-9a-f]{8}\b=, scan the footnote block, block if any cited id is unfootnoted. This is the project's standing answer to a convention that recurs -- the same instinct as =bin/lint-org= and the =org-trigger-hook= guards.
3. *Make compliance cheap.* The real cost is looking up an exact title per id, which is why the discipline degrades exactly when a response cites five of them. [[id:478d6ec9-1b99-4c6c-88e9-c793ad4b2f22][478d6ec9]] and its child [[id:2d9eeebd-4375-4790-8485-92b244236a21][2d9eeebd]] are already this: resolve an 8-character prefix and read front matter without a whole-body read. A batch prefix→title lookup makes footnoting nearly free, and a rule that is free to obey does not need enforcing.

*The one hazard in (2), and it is not incidental:* the docs describe *no* loop guard for a blocking =Stop= hook -- no =stop_hook_active= flag. A hook that blocks on a condition the model then fails to satisfy loops forever. It needs its own sentinel, one fire per turn, on the model of the =block-open= file [[id:f4e628ce-e653-429a-8f3c-0f5d4241b139][f4e628ce]] already uses.

*Related but a different surface:* [[id:d2a0f54c-b59a-4855-ae3d-c93a6d3aa177][d2a0f54c]] generalises convention checking over *files* at pre-commit. This one checks *response prose* at turn end. Same instinct, no shared code.

*Shipped 2026-08-24* (=9fcc08b=): =bin/hooks/footnote-check=, wired to =Stop=, with =bin/footnote-check-test= covering it. 8 of 8 pass.

*Reads =last_assistant_message=, not =transcript_path=.* The hook docs are explicit that the transcript is written asynchronously and may lag the in-memory conversation, so a hook needing the current turn's final text must use that field. Reading the transcript would have checked the *previous* response and blocked on the wrong thing -- a bug that would have looked like flakiness rather than like a mistake.

*Candidates are validated against the org files rather than pattern-matched.* An 8-hex token is not necessarily an =:ID:=; a git short hash is the obvious collision and this repo's prose is full of them. Only tokens appearing as =:ID:  <token>= in a tracked file are enforced, which turns a heuristic into a lookup and makes a false positive nearly impossible. Two of the eight tests pin that: =deadbeef= and an 8-digit number are both ignored.

*The loop guard is load-bearing, not defensive.* The docs describe no =stop_hook_active= flag, so a hook that blocks on a condition the model then fails to satisfy blocks forever. It fires at most once per turn via a per-session sentinel on the =block-open= model, written when it blocks and cleared by =bin/hooks/session-resume= when the next prompt starts a new turn. Clearing it from the =Stop= hook itself would reintroduce the loop exactly.

*The first draft failed every case that should have blocked,* and the cause is already on record here: =mapfile=, an unguarded ="${arr[@]}"= under =set -u=, and array append -- all bash 4, while macOS ships 3.2 and =#!/usr/bin/env bash= resolves to it. The same three constructs, in the same order, that [[id:7e9aa4ea-4757-4e58-bac3-3c3e67fc4cb1][7e9aa4ea]] recorded hitting. Rewritten for 3.2. Worth noting the tests caught it immediately, which is the only reason it cost minutes rather than a silently non-firing feature -- the failure mode [[id:f4e628ce-e653-429a-8f3c-0f5d4241b139][f4e628ce]] lived in for weeks.

*Two failures measured while testing were my test's fault, not the code's,* and both were the same mistake: =VAR=x cmd1 | cmd2= scopes VAR to =cmd1= only, so the hook under test read the default queue directory and appeared not to clear its sentinel. Recorded because it will recur -- every hook here takes its payload on stdin through a pipe, so the natural way to write the test is the wrong one.

*Known interaction, accepted rather than engineered around:* a blocked turn fires =Stop= twice, so =session-pause= writes two =pause= guideposts with no =resume= between them. The second is the true turn end; the first does not pair, since =--span-work-runs= only pairs =resume= -> =pause=. So a span ends where it should and the cost is one unpaired point.

*Not live in this session.* Hook configuration is read once at session start, so the new =Stop= entry takes effect in a new session only. This heading's own claim to work cannot be verified until then -- the first real test is whether the next session's responses carry their footnotes.

*Still worth doing, and it is the half that removes the problem rather than policing it:* [[id:478d6ec9-1b99-4c6c-88e9-c793ad4b2f22][478d6ec9]] and [[id:2d9eeebd-4375-4790-8485-92b244236a21][2d9eeebd]] make a prefix→title lookup cheap. A rule that is free to obey does not need enforcing, and the enforcement exists because looking up five exact titles mid-response is not free today.


*** TODO The footnote hook's stopReason never reaches the model; the block is opaque :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       5787bfc6-3ba5-4d94-802c-f8a7879de68d
:CREATED:  [2026-08-25 Tue 11:57]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-25 Tue 11:57] \\
  observed twice; the hook blocked and said nothing
:END:

*Observed 2026-08-25, twice in one session.* The hook fired and the text that reached the model was exactly =Stop hook stopped continuation: Stop hook prevented continuation= -- no ids, no instruction, none of the =stopReason= the hook composes. The model continued without correcting anything, because it had nothing to correct *from*.

*The hook itself is fine, and that was checked rather than assumed.* Fed a body citing two tracked ids with one footnoted, it emits well-formed JSON naming the missing one and carrying the full instruction. Both real blocks were genuine violations -- =46e4ce2b= and =98c302e0= cited and unfootnoted in one response, =8ca6541d= and =aa1ba915= in another. So the *detection* half is verified in production; it is the delivery half that is missing.

*Why that matters more than it sounds.* A gate that blocks without saying why converts a corrective nudge into an unexplained stall: the turn ends, the model is re-invoked, and the violation is still there. The loop guard means it costs one extra continuation and never hangs, so the failure is quiet -- which is how it survived two occurrences without being noticed as a defect rather than as the feature working.

*What is not yet known* is where the text is lost. Candidates, none tested: =stopReason= may be surfaced to the *user* rather than to the model; the field may need to sit somewhere other than =hookSpecificOutput= for a blocking Stop hook; or the harness may summarise any block to a fixed string. Worth checking against the hook documentation before changing the hook, since the JSON shape currently matches what was designed from those docs.

*Blocks judgement on* the parent [[id:2758f3a0-3c47-405e-9662-a7669dcb60cc][2758f3a0]], which is in =REVIEW= awaiting exactly this evidence: it fires, it is accurate, and it cannot yet tell anyone what it found.


** TODO The :PLAN: lint will demand a drawer from a debrief-only heading :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       f421c5c3-f81f-4ebf-99c9-42c53cc00558
:CREATED:  [2026-08-24 Mon 15:02]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- [2026-08-24 Mon 14:53]--[2026-08-24 Mon 14:58] assigned at review
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-24 Mon 15:02] \\
  captured keywordless, setting initial state
:END:

*Latent, not firing yet, and found 2026-08-24 by running the sweep rather than by reading the rule.*

[[id:8bcd56f4-22a8-4673-bad6-a99433027b81][8bcd56f4]]'s lint rule warns when a heading closed on or after 2026-08-24 has a substantial body and no =:PLAN:= drawer. It assumes every finished heading has a prospective half to wrap. *Six do not.*

Those six refused =org_wrap_plan= with =Seam marker is the first body line; nothing would be wrapped= -- their bodies are *entirely debrief*, written outcome-first since the convention landed. A heading written after the convention often has nothing to wrap, which is the convention working, not a heading missing a step.

*Only a threshold is keeping the rule quiet.* =--lint-substantial-body-p= requires ten prose lines and those six carry five to eight. A debrief-only heading with a longer outcome -- and this project writes long outcomes -- would warn, and the only way to satisfy the warning would be to wrap a debrief into a drawer readers are told to skip. That is the inversion the whole lifecycle exists to prevent, arrived at by obeying the lint.

*The signal already exists and is cheap.* =claude-code-ide-org--plan-seam='s "first body line" condition is exactly the test for "no prospective half", so the rule can ask before it warns rather than inferring from body length. A body whose first line is an outcome marker needs no drawer.

*Not fixed now* because it fires on nothing today, and a rule changed while quiet cannot be seen to improve. Worth doing before the next long-bodied heading closes.


* Review and planning
:PROPERTIES:
:DATE_TREE: t
:ARCHIVE:  DONE.org::* Review and planning
:END:

Meta-work: the review pass, planning, and the time that goes into deciding what to do rather than doing it. Time is clocked against the *day node*, which is why the day node carries an =:ID:= and the year and month nodes above it do not -- they are org-datetree's own scaffolding, and =bin/lint-org= knows the difference ([[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]).

=:DATE_TREE:= is what makes org nest the tree *inside* this category instead of writing a second =* 2026= at level 1; it is a structural property about the grouping, permitted on a category on the same footing as =:ARCHIVE:=. The =:ARCHIVE:= target is matched as a literal string, so renaming this category means renaming the matching heading in DONE.org in the same edit.


** TODO Open today's node
SCHEDULED: <2026-08-24 Mon 07:00 ++1d>
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       2aff1e6d-71cf-4b3f-8a3f-b3fc63d07c99
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]
:END:

The ritual: a standing daily prompt to actually do review and planning.

*It reminds; it does not create.* Creating today's node is [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]]'s job and happens when work happens. A repeater that created the node instead would manufacture one per calendar day whether or not anything occurred, and re-couple the node's id to a schedule -- both of the things the on-demand resolver exists to avoid.

*A category is the one place in this repo a repeater is safe* ([[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]]): a repeater never reaches DONE, so it can never sit under a completable ancestor, and a category carries no keyword and completes nothing.

The recurring pass: archive what is closed, so the live file stops carrying it.

*What to do,* in order -- the reasons are in =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= under "Archiving", not here:

1. Apply the queue.
2. Consolidate every drawer ([[id:7ae6562d-25c8-453e-b78b-920e036c5665][7ae6562d]]).
3. Archive every =DONE= *and* =CANCELLED= heading that carries an outcome summary.

Paired with [[id:2aff1e6d-71cf-4b3f-8a3f-b3fc63d07c99][2aff1e6d]] at the same timestamp so the two land as one sitting. =SCHEDULED= not =DEADLINE=, and =++1d= not =+1d=: the first says the time is a suggestion, the second means a missed day is skipped rather than banked.

*Step 1 cannot be delegated* -- apply is human-triggered by design -- which bounds what any scheduler can do here. [[id:aa1ba915-ee68-48df-afea-197ae60aac63][aa1ba915]] is the prompt; it can prepare and remind, not perform.

*The existing backlog is [[id:7fbab3b3-7843-4290-94a5-d421413f2be4][7fbab3b3]], not this heading.* Clearing 36 accumulated headings is a one-time migration with a decision attached; this is the steady state after it.

/Body pared 2026-08-24, from 47 lines. It had grown to hold three different things -- this ritual, that backlog, and a set of standing conventions -- and a repeater never reaches/ =DONE=/, so nothing in the/ =:PLAN:= /lifecycle would ever have collected it. See/ [[id:ff92700e-1713-4862-a3fe-8fd972ad96fa][ff92700e]]./
