:END:

*Split out of [[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]] on 2026-08-24*, which had been carrying three different things at once: a recurring ritual, this one-time migration, and a set of standing conventions. The conventions went to =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md=; the ritual stayed; this is the backlog.

*The first pass is a backlog, not a cadence.* Clearing the existing 36 should not wait for the repeater -- the schedule is for the steady state afterwards. Re-measured 2026-08-24 against both terminal keywords: *36* closed headings in TODO.org, 35 =DONE= and 1 =CANCELLED=. (An earlier count of 30 was =DONE=-only, which is exactly the omission that makes a =DONE=-filtered sweep strand abandoned work.) [[id:4c0044bf-1b6c-44ea-807a-8f9e3523e3fd][4c0044bf]] made the same observation on 2026-08-07 at 13 headings and was cancelled partly for it.

*A direct cost, not housekeeping.* Those bodies are read into context by every session that opens the tracker, which is [[id:b07df584-3adc-433c-ab49-e104f88aecf7][b07df584]]'s complaint with a number attached.

*DECIDED 2026-08-21 by the user: sweep the old bodies into =:PLAN:= unedited.* The backlog does not get the faithful paring a newly written heading will; the existing body is wrapped as-is and the debrief written beneath it. That splits the migration into one mechanical step and one cheap one. *Wrapping* is now a tool -- =org_wrap_plan=, shipped 2026-08-24 -- and needs no judgement at all. The *debrief* is still per heading, but far cheaper than the [[id:cc0c17a7-a554-426d-afeb-09cbee7339f0][cc0c17a7]] trial suggested: the expensive judgement there was deciding what to *discard*, and sweeping discards nothing.

*Consequence, accepted rather than overlooked:* old drawers will be raw transcripts where new ones are pared plans. The ignore-the-drawer rule covers both, so the inconsistency is invisible in normal reading, and any heading touched again can be pared then.

*Why it stalls, and what changed.* CLAUDE.md requires a prose outcome summary on a =DONE= heading before it is archived, so this was never mechanical. That is still true and is the whole remaining cost -- but the two things that used to block it are gone: the wrapping tool exists, and the ordering it depends on is written down in the rules file rather than in a task body nobody would find.

*Sequence, from the rules file:* apply the queue, consolidate every drawer ([[id:7ae6562d-25c8-453e-b78b-920e036c5665][7ae6562d]]), then archive. Never archive first.

*Note the lint will start reporting these as they close.* =bin/lint-org= now warns when a heading closed on or after 2026-08-24 has a substantial body and no =:PLAN:= drawer. The 36 here are all closed before that date and are deliberately exempt, so the backlog stays silent rather than shouting 124 findings a day.


** TODO [0/3] Make the daily ceremony cheap enough that it actually runs :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       edd47f32-e6c9-4ab3-8e8a-46a37ea29605
:CREATED:  [2026-08-24 Mon 15:36]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-25 Tue 10:03] \\
  never had an initial state set
:END:

*The story these three share, noticed 2026-08-24 while surveying what the session had captured.* They were filed separately, into three different categories, and each reads as a small independent defect. Together they are one thing: *the ceremony has four steps, two of them have a tool, and nothing composes them or reminds anyone.*

The sequence, from =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md=: *apply the queue, consolidate every drawer, then archive* -- plus re-separating headings, which applying always disturbs.

| step | state |
|--------------------------+-------------------------------------------|
| apply the queue | human-only by design, and permanently so |
| consolidate the drawers | [[id:7ae6562d-25c8-453e-b78b-920e036c5665][7ae6562d]] -- not built |
| re-separate the headings | [[id:e1284bdb-0a18-402a-81cb-1ac8d09fb8a2][e1284bdb]] -- built, must be re-run by hand |
| archive | manual |
| *remember to do any of it* | [[id:aa1ba915-ee68-48df-afea-197ae60aac63][aa1ba915]] -- not built |

*The shape of the problem is not "these are unbuilt".* It is that each one only helps if a human remembers it at the right moment, and the moment is the same moment for all of them. A normaliser that must be recalled separately from the pass that disturbs the file is a normaliser that will drift -- measured on [[id:7ae6562d-25c8-453e-b78b-920e036c5665][7ae6562d]] itself, where 13 of 25 drawers archived since consolidate-on-apply shipped are still out of order, and observed three times today with the separation normaliser, which had to be re-run after every apply.

*Why the ritual heading is not a child of this one, though it is the same subject.* [[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]] carries a repeater, and =bin/lint-org= refuses a repeater under a completable ancestor -- correctly, since such an ancestor could never reach =DONE=. So the ritual stays where it is and this heading covers only the *tooling that makes the ritual cheap*. That constraint is worth stating: it is why an obvious "everything about the ceremony" subtree is not the right shape.

*Done when* a human runs one command, answers the prompts apply asks, and the file comes out consolidated, separated and archived -- with the prompt to do so arriving on its own.

*This heading carried no TODO keyword until 2026-08-25*, and the user asked whether that was deliberate. It was not: there is no =:LOGBOOK:= on it at all, so no state was ever set. It was written directly as a parent in =4b115d8=, rather than captured and then given an initial state, which is how every other container heading here got one. Seven of eight carry a keyword; this was the only exception.

*The exception falls exactly on the emergent/declared axis*, which is why the question was worth asking rather than just fixing. The other seven all *acquired* their children -- emergent epics, detectable and never declared. This one was created to bundle three headings that already existed, filed under three different categories; its opening line says so. So the corpus accidentally encoded "emergent gets a keyword, declared does not", in a sample of one, with nothing written down and no mechanism enforcing it.

*Keywordless is not a defensible marker for a declared grouping, though, and the reason is mechanical.* A story composed by reference is a checkbox list blocked on its members ([[id:8ca6541d-0fc7-45a2-a4d3-76e1608f658d][8ca6541d]]), and =org-depend= blocks on an *unfinished TODO keyword*. A member with no keyword can never be unfinished, so a checkbox naming it is inert -- the same inertness CLAUDE.md already records for a =:BLOCKER:= written in the session that captured its target. Found while the user was drafting exactly such a list against this heading, which is the only reason it surfaced at all.

*Set to =TODO= rather than =DOING=, deliberately.* A transition to =DOING= obliges a clock, and a container's own clock measures nothing -- the work clocks on [[id:aa1ba915-ee68-48df-afea-197ae60aac63][aa1ba915]], which is the child actually in progress.

*The cost of the omission was narrower than assumed, and worth recording so it is not overstated later.* =org_outline= with =active_only= keeps a keywordless heading, since the filter drops only =DONE= and =CANCELLED= -- verified against this subtree. What it actually forfeits is narrower: it can never be nominated =NEXT=, never appears in a TODO agenda, and its =[0/3]= cookie can never reach =DONE=.



*** TODO Consolidation only reaches drawers an apply pass happens to touch :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7ae6562d-25c8-453e-b78b-920e036c5665
:CREATED:  [2026-08-24 Mon 09:20]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-24 Mon 09:21] \\
  captured keywordless, setting initial state
:END:

*Measured 2026-08-24, over both files.* =claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-on-apply= is =t= and works -- but it only fires on a heading that an apply pass *writes to*. A drawer disturbed by anything else keeps its disorder, and archiving ends the only thing that would have repaired it.

Counting drawers with two or more timestamped entries, and asking whether their entries ascend:

| file                     | drifted | of  |
|--------------------------+---------+-----|
| TODO.org                 |       9 |  62 |
| DONE.org                 |      43 |  60 |

*The split by =:ARCHIVE_TIME:= is what makes it a finding rather than a backlog.* Consolidation-on-apply landed 2026-08-13, so anything archived before then is expected to be disordered and is not evidence. Anything archived after is:

| archived            | drifted | of |
|---------------------+---------+----|
| before 2026-08-13   |       6 |  6 |
| on/after 2026-08-13 |      13 | 25 |
| no =:ARCHIVE_TIME:= |      24 | 29 |

*Over half of everything archived since the feature shipped is out of order*, which is not what "consolidate on apply" implies to a reader of the defcustom.

*Cause, and it is structural rather than a bug.* Org inserts =CLOCK:= lines newest-first; =--append-to-drawer= appends annotations before =:END:=; the ascending order [[id:af7d3687-6d1e-4480-a9e2-df772785713f][af7d3687]] chose is neither. So a drawer drifts the moment anything writes to it outside apply -- a hand =C-c C-x C-i=, or =--trigger-auto-clock-in= firing on a hand-set =DOING=. TODO.org stays healthy only because apply keeps returning to those headings. Once a heading is archived, nothing ever touches it again and the drift is permanent.

*Why this rather than reversing the sort.* Newest-first would end the drift by construction, by agreeing with org's insert convention. It was considered and rejected: it reverses the narrative order that was the whole of af7d3687's request, it breaks that heading's byte-identical fixed-point test against =clock-template.org=, and it systematically flips every tie whose adjacency the stable sort was chosen to preserve. The drift is a *coverage* problem, not an *ordering* one, and the coverage fix keeps what was asked for.

*Shape:* a command that consolidates every drawer in the tracked files, reusing =--consolidate-logbook-text= unchanged -- already idempotent and pinned lossless. Worth running before an archive pass in particular, since that is the last moment a heading is ever touched; that makes it a natural companion to [[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]] rather than a separate ritual.


*** NEXT End every heading with two blank lines, so folded trees are separated :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e1284bdb-0a18-402a-81cb-1ac8d09fb8a2
:CREATED:  [2026-08-24 Mon 13:12]
:END:

*Shipped 2026-08-24* (=b6619e5=). Every heading's content now ends with exactly two blank lines before the next heading.

*Two, and the number is not a matter of taste.* Org hides the blank line between a collapsed subtree and the following headline unless there are at least =org-cycle-separator-lines= of them, and that variable is 2 by default here. Two in the file buys exactly one visible line of air when folded; one buys none. So the convention is calibrated to a setting that already existed, and needed no configuration change.

*Before every heading, not only before same-level ones,* and the corpus settled that. The gap you see before =** B= in a folded outline is the trailing space of A's *last child*, not of A's own body: 7 of 88 level-2 headings are preceded by a deeper heading, and 31 of 134 gaps sit between different levels. A same-level rule would skip exactly those.

*=claude-code-ide-org-normalize-heading-separation= touches only the run of blank lines immediately preceding a heading*, found by walking back from the next heading and stopping at the first non-blank line. That is the safety property: the edit cannot reach text. Verified on the first run -- 224 insertions, 37 deletions, and *zero* changed lines carrying any character. The last heading in each file is left alone; there is nothing after it to separate from.

*Re-run twice since,* because applying queued events inserts lines without the convention. That is the shape of the thing: it is a normaliser, not a one-time migration, and it belongs in the same sitting as the consolidation sweep [[id:7ae6562d-25c8-453e-b78b-920e036c5665][7ae6562d]] rather than being remembered separately.

*Not yet written down as a convention anywhere* -- neither =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= nor CLAUDE.md mentions it, so nothing tells a session to preserve it and no lint asserts it. That is this heading's remaining work.


*** DOING Prompt the daily ceremony automatically, within what a scheduler can reach :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       aa1ba915-ee68-48df-afea-197ae60aac63
:CREATED:  [2026-08-24 Mon 10:27]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from              [2026-08-24 Mon 10:28] \\
  captured keywordless, nominating as next
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 11:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:06] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-24 Mon 11:02] \\
  fix stale comment, audit whether the report fires
- [2026-08-24 Mon 11:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:06] audit the session-start report path
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 11:14]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:15] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-24 Mon 11:14]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:15] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 11:17]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:18] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-24 Mon 11:17]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:18] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 11:26]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:29] =>  0:03
- [2026-08-24 Mon 11:26]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:29] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 11:44]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:46] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-24 Mon 11:44]--[2026-08-24 Mon 11:46] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:00]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:08] =>  0:08
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:00]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:08] suggested span, accepted at review
:END:

Asked 2026-08-24: can the daily ceremony ([[id:cbe282ec-10c3-4aa0-8d3a-f30e17a12fa8][cbe282ec]], now =<... 07:00 ++1d>=) be scheduled in the agentic CLI so a prompt arrives at the first opportunity each day and the agent performs it?

*Measured against the actual tool surface, not assumed.* Two schedulers exist and neither fits:

- =CronCreate= is *session-only* -- in-memory, nothing written to disk, gone when the session exits, recurring jobs auto-expire after 7 days, and jobs fire only while the REPL is idle. A standing daily ritual outlives any session, so this is the wrong shape by construction rather than by degree.
- The =schedule= skill creates *cloud* routines. A cloud agent cannot reach =localhost:45571=, and every MCP tool in this project goes through =emacsclient= to a local Emacs server. It could not perform a single step.

*And the first step of the ceremony cannot be delegated at all.* Apply is human-triggered by design: org's native state-change logging only completes inside a genuinely interactive command. So "performed by the agent" is unavailable for the pass as a whole no matter which scheduler is used. What is available is *prompting* plus the steps after apply -- consolidate and archive.

*The mechanism this repo already has is the right one, and it is the =SessionStart= hook.* =bin/hooks/session-start-recovery-check= already fires at session start, already injects =additionalContext= that the session relays to the user as a question, and is already *self-limiting to first thing each day* -- it reports only intervals opened before today, so it goes quiet once handled. That is precisely the requested behaviour: a prompt at the first opportunity each day, no nagging, and nothing at all on a day with no session.

It also already has the right manners for this: [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]] established that the report *asks and never proposes*. A ceremony line should follow that -- state what is waiting (pending item count, headings ready to archive, drawers out of order) and let the human decide, rather than announcing an intention to act.

*The one design question to settle before building:* what counts as "already done today". Candidates -- a stamp file, on the model of the =block-open= sentinel; the existence of a day node for today; or the pending-queue count having dropped since the last pass. The stamp file is the cheapest and the only one that does not conflate "ceremony performed" with "work happened".

*Not to be confused with a nag.* The org repeater already makes the item show up in the agenda daily. This heading is only about the *agentic* prompt, for the case where the agenda is not what the user is looking at.

*Audited 2026-08-24, prompted by the user asking whether the report has ever actually asked them anything.* It has not, and that is correct behaviour rather than a fault.

*The mechanism works.* Verified today by running a synthetic stale interval through the real path in batch Emacs: =--session-start-hook-json= returns well-formed =hookSpecificOutput= / =additionalContext=, and the text does ask what time work stopped while explicitly forbidding the relaying session to propose one. So this is *not* [[id:c5b02503-1700-47fd-bf89-bd3296b24af2][c5b02503]] -- the report was never suppressed in relaying, it was never generated.

*Its single condition has never held.* The report fires only for an unclosed =CLOCK:= line whose open timestamp *predates today*. Scanning every commit that touched TODO.org: unclosed lines were routine between 2026-07-28 and 2026-08-17, and *every one of them was closed the same day it opened*. Not one survived overnight.

*Two things see to that.* =kill-emacs-hook= and =suspend-hook= both run =claude-code-ide-org--clock-out-if-clocking=, so a clean quit or a sleep closes an open clock -- which covers everything short of a crash or power loss. And since the 2026-08-11 cutover =org_clock_in= queues rather than opening a clock at all, so the only live clocks left come from a human clocking by hand or =--trigger-auto-clock-in= firing on a hand-set =DOING=. The feature is a net for an unclean death, not a routine check.

*A correction worth keeping, because the first answer was the opposite one.* The initial scan reported overnight-stale intervals throughout, up to and including today. It was wrong: the pattern matched =CLOCK: [date= without anchoring the end of the line, and a *closed* line begins identically -- =CLOCK: [start]--[end] => H:MM=. So every closed interval from a previous day counted as an open one. Anchoring with =$= reversed the finding completely. The same shape of mistake as [[id:f4b07fc0-187a-4975-aa3b-b9bd4b1c0799][f4b07fc0]]'s prose-quoting one, hours apart: a pattern that matches more than it names, over text where the extra matches look exactly like real hits.

*Comment corrected in* =bin/hooks/session-start-recovery-check=. It still described injecting "an educated guess ... based on =claude-code-ide-org-working-hours=", retired 2026-08-14 with [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]], and still mentioned =:SESSIONS:=, retired 2026-08-11. The behaviour was right; only the comment lied. It now also says to *expect silence*, with the audit's reason -- so the next reader does not go looking for a bug in a feature that is working.
